# Phase 5 Build Progress Cache

Read this file first at the start of any session touching Phase 5. It is the authoritative "where did we leave off" record — update it immediately after every agent pass (success OR failure/interruption), not just at module completion, so a mid-module crash still leaves an accurate resume point.

Module order/scope: see `docs/phase-5/00-module-plan.md`. Execution rule: build all 21 continuously, no per-module approval gate (only Phase 5→6 is a gate).

## Environment status

- pnpm: installed globally. Node v25.
- **Docker Desktop: WSL2 installed by the user, containers running.** `docker-compose.dev.yml` (postgres:16 + redis:7) is up and healthy on `localhost:5432`/`localhost:6379`. A root `.env` exists pointing at them. **Phase 5 has now been fully verified against this real database — see "Phase 5 Full Verification" section below.**
- Git: no repository initialized yet in this project.

Legend: entities/repos → application services → api (controllers+DTOs+Swagger) → events → tests → migration → seed-extended (0900) → verified (tsc+eslint+test green)

**PASS A (application layer, this pass)**: `domains/payments/application/` — `CashierSessionsService` (`openSession()`/`closeSession()`/`getOpenSessionForCashier()`, own transaction via injected `DataSource`, same top-level-entry-point shape as `ConcessionsService`; BR-PAY-04's `uq_pay_session_open_p` unique-violation translated to `ConflictException`; `closeSession()` derives per-method expected totals from this session's `POSTED` receipts' splits — re-aggregated at close time, never maintained incrementally, deliberately avoiding a second hot-row on every receipt capture — compares against a caller-supplied `counted` map, sums each method's absolute discrepancy into one `variance_amount`, and requires a `CloseSessionApproval` (supervisorId+reason, BR-PAY-05) once that exceeds Settings key `payments.session_variance_tolerance` (default `0.00`, FR-PAY-011.1)), `AllocationService.resolveAllocations()` (BR-PAY-02/03 — `OLDEST_FIRST` fully implemented against `BillInvoiceRepository.findOpenForStudent()`'s already-due-date-sorted result, greedy consumption per invoice, any remainder always becomes one `{toPrepayment:true}` entry so a receipt can never leave money unaccounted for; `CATEGORY_PRIORITY` throws a clear "not yet implemented" `ValidationException` rather than silently mis-allocating — documented future enhancement, Settings key `billing.allocation_default_rule`), and `ReceiptsService` (THE core engine — `captureReceipt()`/`reverseReceipt()`, both composable over the caller's own `EntityManager` like `InvoicingService`).

**`captureReceipt()` algorithm** (12 steps, see the service's own doc comment for full detail): idempotency replay (`uq_pay_receipt_idempotency_key`, no second effect) → validate student/splits (Σsplits=total BR-PAY-01, per-method mandatory refs, WALLET rejected outright) → BR-PAY-04 session check for CASH splits → CHEQUE splits create `pay_cheque` (UNCLEARED) rows → `AllocationService.resolveAllocations()` → ONE `PostingService.post()` call realizing P-08 (clearing-account debit per method, aggregated by resolved GL account; AR-Student credit for the invoice-allocated portion) + P-09 (same clearing debit pool; Student-prepayments credit for the `toPrepayment` portion) as one balanced journal → `NumberingService.allocate(em,'PAY_RECEIPT')` → insert `pay_receipt`+`pay_receipt_split`+`pay_receipt_allocation` rows (`balance_after` computed arithmetically from the student's pre-transaction running ledger balance minus `total`, since `trg_pay_receipt_immutable` freezes it after INSERT so it must be correct at insert time — no second-query update) → apply each invoice-scoped allocation to `bill_invoice.paid_amount`/`.balance`/`.status` (the SAME lever `ConcessionsService.postStandalone()`/`CreditNotesService` already use against an already-POSTED invoice — reused directly via `BillInvoiceRepository`, already exported from `domains/billing`'s barrel, rather than asking billing to grow a new method for a 3-line pattern with an existing precedent) plus `bill_installment.settled_amount` (oldest-`seq`-first, a documented simplification — `pay_receipt_allocation` rows stay invoice-scoped, installment settlement is pure `ReceiptsService`-side bookkeeping) → append one `std_ledger_entry` (credit=total) → cashier session totals are NOT touched here (derived at close time, see `CashierSessionsService` above).

**Control-account resolution** (the genuinely ambiguous part, task brief's explicit judgement call): new `application/payment-clearing-accounts.util.ts` `resolveClearingAccount()` — `MPESA_STK`/`MPESA_C2B`/`MPESA_TILL` → `control_domain=MPESA_CLEARING`; `BANK_TRANSFER` → `control_domain=TRANSFER_CLEARING` (both via billing's `resolveControlAccount()`, imported from `domains/billing`'s barrel since it's already exported there — no new billing surface needed); `CASH`/`BANK`/`CHEQUE`/`CARD`/`POS` have no dedicated `control_domain` in the DDL, so resolved by plain `gl_account.code` lookup against a small hardcoded map (`CASH→1010`, `BANK→1020` — both already in the `0900` seed; `CHEQUE→1030`, `CARD`/`POS→1040` — **not yet seeded**, a documented forward gap for Pass B's seed extension, since this pass does not touch `0900`). AR-Student/Student-prepayments resolved via the same `resolveControlAccount()` (`AR_STUDENT`/`PREPAYMENT` control domains).

**`reverseReceipt()`** (BR-PAY-08): unwinds every allocation exactly (reverses `bill_invoice.paid_amount`/`.balance`, and unwinds `bill_installment.settled_amount` in REVERSE `seq` order — the exact mirror of the forward oldest-seq-first application, an exact undo absent other interleaving settlement activity) → `PostingService.reverse()` on the original journal → generates a contra receipt via a distinct `docType='RVS_PAY_RECEIPT'` (its first 3 characters auto-derive `NumberingService`'s `RVS-` prefix with zero change to `NumberingService` itself) → mirrors the original's splits/allocations onto the contra row (satisfies `trg_pay_splits_sum`/`trg_pay_allocations_sum`'s deferred constraints for the new row) → sets original `status='REVERSED'`+`reversal_reason`+`approval_ref`, contra's `reversal_of_id` is the cross-reference (`PayReceiptRepository.findByReversalOfId()` added for the original-side lookup, since `pay_receipt` carries no forward-pointing column). Does NOT call `ApprovalEngineService` itself — accepts a pre-approved `approvalRef` and records it, matching `ConcessionsService.requestConcession()` (submits) vs `.postStandalone()` (assumes already-approved); Pass B wires the `PAYMENT_REVERSALS` chain at the controller/workflow level.

`payments.module.ts` now imports `AccountingModule`/`SettingsModule`/`StudentsModule`/`BillingModule` (not just entities), same ordering discipline (own entities before sibling-module barrels) Module 9 documented. `payments/index.ts` barrel extended with all 3 services + types. Out of scope, correctly deferred to Pass B: WALLET-method splits (rejected with a clear error — Module 11/Wallet not built), M-Pesa transaction linkage validation (`mpesaTransactionId` accepted as an unvalidated pass-through field), the `PAYMENT_REVERSALS` approval-chain wiring, cheque clearing, M-Pesa STK/C2B/B2C, suspense matching, bulk allocation, ALL controllers, permission catalogue, `0900` seed. No controllers/`api/`/permission-catalogue/seed changes were made in this pass. 33 new unit tests (one spec per service, thorough posting-map assertions — exact debit/credit account+amount pairs for P-08/P-09, surplus-to-prepayment, mixed CASH+MPESA_STK, cheque-split `pay_cheque` creation, idempotency replay, WALLET rejection, session-required-for-CASH, BR-PAY-03 no-remainder assertion, reversal unwind incl. installment reverse-seq unwind) — 535/535 total passing, tsc clean, eslint clean. **Did NOT build**: `api/`/controllers, permission codes, or `0900` seed changes — all deferred to Pass B (M-Pesa STK/C2B/B2C handling, suspense matching, cheque clearing, bulk bank-statement allocation, ALL controllers, permission catalogue, seed, integration tests). |

`NUMERIC(18,6)` weighted-average-cost columns (`inv_item.avg_cost`, `inv_movement.unit_cost`, `inv_transfer_line.unit_cost`) are **also** deliberately left un-Money-transformed — `Money` (`shared/money/money.ts`) is hard-coded to `SCALE = 4`, one digit coarser than the DDL's own deliberate extra precision for weighted-average cost accumulation, so routing these through `MoneyTransformer` would silently truncate the schema's own intent; ordinary `NUMERIC(18,4)` money columns (`inv_stock_balance.value`, `inv_movement.value`, `inv_item.sale_price`, `inv_stock_take_line.variance_value`) use `MoneyTransformer`/`RequiredMoneyTransformer` as normal. `inv_movement.department_id` stays a loose nullable uuid with **no FK** — the source DDL's own shorthand gives it no `→` arrow (unlike `item_id →`/`store_id →` on the same line), read as a deliberate DDL choice, not an oversight. `preferred_supplier_ids uuid[]` stays a loose array with no FK regardless of `domains/procurement`'s presence in the dependency graph — a Postgres array cannot carry a real FK constraint at all, a structural limitation (same treatment `appr_level.user_ids` gets), not a scoping gap. **BR-INV-03 (stock-take freeze between snapshot and posting) is documented as a SERVICE-layer concern, not a DB trigger** — `inv_stock_take.scope` (jsonb, an arbitrary item/category/store selector) makes a generic DB-level "block movements on counted items" trigger impractical, the same class of judgement call BR-PROC-04's "allocation ≤ invoice open balance" half made in migration `0100`; the next pass's stock-take service must check open stock-takes' `scope` before permitting a movement against a frozen item/store. `domains/inventory/infrastructure/` — 9 plain repositories plus the forward-looking finders the task brief named: `InvStockBalanceRepository.findByIdForUpdate(em, itemId, storeId)` (pessimistic row lock, the load-bearing method the next pass's weighted-average stock-movement engine will use, mirroring `WallWalletRepository.findByIdForUpdate()`'s exact discipline), `InvItemRepository.searchByName()` (trgm, mirrors `ProcSupplierRepository.searchByName()`)/`.findByBarcode()`, `InvMovementRepository.listForItemStore()`. `inventory.module.ts`/`index.ts` — deliberately minimal (entities + repositories only, mirroring what Procurement's own foundation pass registered before its application-layer pass added sibling-module imports/controllers), no `application/`/`api/`/`events/`. Migration `0110-create-inventory-tables.ts` — all 9 tables + `ix_inv_item_name_trgm` (GIN trgm on `name`) + `ix_inv_item_barcode` (partial unique, non-NULL barcodes) + `ix_inv_movement_item_store_at`/`ix_inv_movement_at_brin` (BRIN, append-only time axis) + all CHECK constraints (BR-INV-01's `qty >= 0` on `inv_stock_balance`, BR-INV-04's resale-item CHECK on `inv_item`) + the `preferred_supplier_ids uuid[]` array column + `inv_stock_take_line.variance_qty` as a real `GENERATED ALWAYS AS (counted_qty - snapshot_qty) STORED` column in raw SQL, mirroring migration `0065`'s `StdStudentEntity.searchName` technique character-for-character + **`trg_inv_movement_immutable`** (`BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` on `inv_movement`, unconditional reject, no exceptions — inventory valuation integrity depends on this ledger never being edited in place, so unlike `wall_transaction`/`gl_journal_line` whose immutability is often just a documented application-layer convention, `inv_movement` genuinely warrants an explicit DB trigger; closest real precedent is `trg_gl_journal_immutable`, migration `0060`). **Writer-guard-trigger judgement call**: no `trg_gl_writer_guard`-style `application_name`-checking trigger was added to `inv_stock_balance`/`inv_movement` — the same deliberate judgement call Wallet's migration `0090` made (documented in full in migration `0110`'s own doc comment): exactly one future service will ever write these two tables, so a fan-in choke point designed for GL's genuine multi-module-writer problem would be over-engineering here; defense-in-depth instead comes from the CHECK constraints, `trg_inv_movement_immutable`, and the repository's row-lock discipline. Real reversible `down()`. `data-source.ts` updated with all 9 new entities. **Module 12 (Procurement) gap closure**: migration `0111-add-procurement-item-fks.ts` adds real `ON DELETE RESTRICT` FKs for all three columns Module 12's foundation pass flagged as forward references — `proc_requisition_line.item_id`, `proc_quotation_line.item_id`, AND `proc_po_line.item_id` (the task brief named only the first and third; `module-deps.json`'s own Module-12 note had already flagged all three, so all three are closed here) → `inv_item`, all staying nullable (free-text/description-only lines remain valid). Each of `ProcRequisitionLineEntity`/`ProcQuotationLineEntity`/`ProcPoLineEntity` updated with a real `@ManyToOne` to `InvItemEntity`, imported directly from its entity file (`../../inventory/domain/inv-item.entity`), never through `domains/inventory`'s barrel, per this codebase's established circular-DI-avoidance discipline. **This closure finally makes Procurement's dormant P-18 GL-posting branch reachable** (`gl-grn-accounts.util.ts`'s `resolveInventoryControlAccount()`, built correctly in Pass A but unreachable dead code since every `proc_po_line.item_id` was NULL) — flagged clearly for the next pass (Inventory's own application-layer pass) to double-check/exercise: create a PO line against a `STOCK`/`RESALE` item, receive it via GRN, and assert the GRN posts to the `INVENTORY` control account rather than the `5050` fallback. `module-deps.json`: new `domains/inventory` entry (`mayImport: ["shared", "accounting", "platform/settings", "platform/approvals", "platform/users"]`, all entity-only at this foundation-pass stage) plus `domains/procurement`'s own entry extended with `domains/inventory` (entity-only, one-directional — `domains/inventory` does not import `domains/procurement` back). One integration test `inventory-triggers.integration.spec.ts` (9-table reachability check + `trg_inv_movement_immutable` UPDATE/DELETE rejection + a real assertion that `variance_qty` is a genuine Postgres-computed `GENERATED STORED` column, including that a direct write to it is rejected, real INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE against Postgres, self-skips without Docker) — 11 new tests at the time (this foundation pass's own 778/778 milestone, now superseded by the application-layer pass's 846/846 above). `pay()` — ONE `PostingService.post()` call realizing P-25, `NumberingService.allocate(em,'EXP_VOUCHER')`, status PAID); `PettyCashService` (THE core float engine — `createFloat()` starts `balance = ceiling`, a documented "fully funded on creation" judgement call since a zero-balance float has no way to bootstrap its own first replenishment cycle, same "no opening-balance GL entry" gap Module 7's own seed data carries; `spend()` row-locks the float via `findByIdForUpdate()`, BR-EXP-02 balance-floor rejection, decrements balance, creates an `exp_petty_cash_voucher` with `status='APPROVED'` directly — deliberately NO per-voucher approval chain (a float represents pre-delegated spending authority up to `ceiling`) and NO per-voucher GL posting, per FR-EXP-003.1's own wording that only the replenishment posts P-26 for the aggregate spent total; `requestReplenishment()` collects every APPROVED voucher not yet claimed by ANY existing replenishment (a computed set-difference against every prior `exp_replenishment.voucher_ids` array, since no new "claimed" column was added to `exp_petty_cash_voucher` — the DDL's own `voucher_ids uuid[]` column is the intended record), sums them, submits under `PETTY_CASH_REPLENISHMENT`; `onApprovalDecided()`'s rejection path DELETES the replenishment row rather than transitioning it — `exp_replenishment.status`'s 3-value DDL enum (`PENDING_APPROVAL\|APPROVED\|PAID`) has no REJECTED/CANCELLED value, so deletion releases its claimed vouchers back into the unclaimed pool for a future `requestReplenishment()` call to pick up, documented on the method itself; `execute()` — ONE `PostingService.post()` call realizing P-26 (debit `1015 Petty Cash Float`, credit `1020 Bank`), restores `float.balance` by exactly the replenished amount, CAPPED at `ceiling` via real arithmetic (`min(balance+amount, ceiling)`, not a blind reset) so any concurrent spend/ceiling-change between request and execution is respected); `ClaimsService` (`exp_claim`+lines CRUD, DRAFT-only line edits, `DRAFT-<uuid>` placeholder allocated for real at `reimburse()`; `submit()`/`onApprovalDecided()` under `EXPENSE_CLAIMS` — approved->APPROVED, rejected->REJECTED (`exp_claim` DOES have a dedicated status, unlike the voucher/replenishment cases); `reimburse()`'s DIRECT-vs-PAYROLL branching — both aggregate every line's amount per `category.gl_expense_account_id` into one clean debit line per account, DIRECT then credits a `method`-resolved clearing account (real cash, `status='REIMBURSED'` genuinely means paid), PAYROLL credits the new `2040 Staff Reimbursements Payable` liability account instead (an ACCRUAL — the expense is genuinely incurred now, `status='REIMBURSED'` here is a documented naming tension meaning "the accrual is booked", NOT "cash has settled" — **Module 15/Payroll's own real forward-integration point: it must debit this SAME `2040` account when it eventually settles a PAYROLL-routed claim inside a payslip run**, documented on `STAFF_REIMBURSEMENTS_PAYABLE_ACCOUNT_CODE`'s own doc comment, the `0900` seed's Module 14 paragraph, and here); `exp_claim` itself carries no `journal_id` column (unlike `exp_voucher`/`exp_replenishment`), so the posting stays traceable only via `gl_journal.source_doc_type='exp_claim'`, logged rather than silently dropped); `schedule_cron`'s next-run-date approach is a REAL, correct 5-field cron evaluator restricted to exact-value-or-`"*"` per field (no ranges/lists/steps — a deliberate, documented scope restriction rather than a half-implemented full cron grammar), walking forward day-by-day capped at 366 days — genuinely supports the task's own named MONTHLY/WEEKLY use cases plus any other exact day/month/weekday combination). All 8 extend `MutableBaseEntity` (`exp_category`/`exp_petty_cash_float`/`exp_recurring` for genuine post-creation config edits; `exp_voucher`/`exp_petty_cash_voucher`/`exp_replenishment`/`exp_claim` for real status-progression workflow, the `ProcPaymentVoucherEntity`/`bill_invoice` shape). **Two status-enum design decisions** (documented on each entity's own doc comment): `exp_petty_cash_voucher.status` → `DRAFT\|PENDING_APPROVAL\|APPROVED\|CANCELLED` (no `PAID` — a petty cash spend IS paid the moment it clears approval, straight out of the float balance, so `APPROVED` is already the terminal financial state); `exp_claim.status` → `DRAFT\|PENDING_APPROVAL\|APPROVED\|REIMBURSED\|REJECTED\|CANCELLED` (adds `REJECTED` distinct from `CANCELLED`, mirroring `ProcRequisitionEntity`'s own reject-vs-withdraw split). **`exp_claim_line` base-class judgement call**: `MutableBaseEntity`, not `BaseEntity` — a claim's lines are freely added/edited/removed while the parent `exp_claim` sits in `DRAFT` (the claimant assembling their expense list pre-submission), the same genuine post-creation edit window `BillInvoiceLineEntity`/`ProcRequisitionLineEntity` established, diverging from `InvTransferLineEntity`'s `BaseEntity` choice (whose parent has no pre-issue `DRAFT` status at all). Migration `0120-create-expenses-tables.ts` — all 8 tables + BR-EXP-02's `ck_exp_petty_cash_float_balance_range` (`balance >= 0 AND balance <= ceiling`) + 3 triggers (mirroring `trg_bill_invoice_immutable`/`trg_proc_po_immutable`'s column-by-column freeze pattern): `trg_exp_voucher_immutable` (freezes `amount`/`category_id`/`payee_type`/`payee_ref`/`method` once `status IN ('APPROVED','PAID')`, the exact column list the task brief named), `trg_exp_petty_cash_voucher_immutable` (freezes `float_id`/`category_id`/`amount`/`receipt_file_id` once `status='APPROVED'`), `trg_exp_claim_immutable` (freezes `staff_user_id`/`total`/`reimburse_via` once `status IN ('APPROVED','REIMBURSED')`) — all three explicitly NOT given a `trg_gl_writer_guard`-style `application_name` gate, same judgement call Wallet/Inventory already made (only one future service will ever write these tables). `PyrlRunLineEntity`/`PyrlRunLineComponentEntity` → plain `BaseEntity` (the documented divergence, same treatment as `gl_journal_line` — computed wholesale by the future computation engine, frozen by the DB trigger below rather than an optimistic-lock version counter; It backs the two `EXCLUDE USING gist` no-overlap constraints (raw SQL in migration `0130`, TypeORM decorators can't express these, noted on each entity pointing at the migration): `excl_pyrl_employee_assignment_no_overlap` (`employee_id WITH =, daterange(effective_from, effective_to, '[]') WITH &&` — one active salary-structure assignment per employee at any date) and `excl_pyrl_employee_component_no_overlap` — a documented judgement call scoping it to `(employee_id, component_id)`, NOT a literal `employee_id`-only copy of the first pattern, since that would wrongly forbid an employee from holding two DIFFERENT components (e.g. housing + transport allowance) concurrently; both use `'[]'` (both-bounds-inclusive) date-range semantics, also documented as a judgement call (no prior daterange/EXCLUDE precedent existed anywhere in this codebase to follow). **Encrypted-column storage decision**: `pyrl_employee.pay_details`/`bank_name`/`branch`/`account` (the DDL's own "(enc)"/"(enc jsonb)" markers) stay typed `jsonb` (the DDL's own literal column type, honored over switching to `bytea`) — checked both existing precedents (`usr_user.twofa_secret_enc`/`set_integration_config.config_enc` are `bytea`; `set_setting.value` is `jsonb`, storing `encryptToBuffer(...).toString("base64")` from `shared/crypto/aes-gcm.util.ts` when `is_secret=true`) and followed the `jsonb`-of-base64-string shape since it's the one that matches this DDL's explicit column type; all four columns are nullable `unknown`/opaque at the entity level, NULL until the next pass's service layer populates them via the same `aes-gcm.util.ts` primitives — no encryption/decryption logic in this pass. `pyrl_loan.rate NUMERIC(9,6)` follows the `InvItemEntity.avgCost`/`InvMovementEntity.unitCost` `NUMERIC(18,6)` precedent (plain string, no `MoneyTransformer` — `Money`'s hard-coded scale-4 would truncate a percentage-shaped rate); no `NUMERIC(9,6)`-scale-6 rate precedent existed in Settings/Accounting to follow instead, confirmed by search. `infrastructure/` — 13 plain repositories plus the forward-looking finders the next passes need immediately: `PyrlStatutoryTableRepository.findEffectiveFor(kind, periodEndDate)` (BR-PYRL-01's exact "latest `effective_from <= periodEndDate`" lookup), `PyrlEmployeeAssignmentRepository.findActiveFor(employeeId, date)`, `PyrlRunRepository.findCommittedMainForPeriod(periodKey)` (BR-PYRL-02), `PyrlEmployeeRepository.searchByName()` (trgm, mirrors `ProcSupplierRepository.searchByName()`). `payroll.module.ts`/`index.ts` — deliberately minimal (`TypeOrmModule.forFeature()` + repositories only, no sibling-module imports since every cross-module FK this pass declares is entity-only, no runtime *service* call yet), mirroring what Module 12 (Procurement)'s own foundation pass registered before its later PASS A added sibling-module imports/controllers. Migration `0130-create-payroll-tables.ts` (after `0125` enables `btree_gist`) — all 13 tables matching the entities exactly, the two `EXCLUDE USING gist` constraints, the `uq_pyrl_main_run_p` partial unique index (BR-PYRL-02), `uq(kind, effective_from)` on `pyrl_statutory_table`, GIN trgm on `pyrl_employee.full_name`, all CHECK enums, plus two triggers: **`trg_pyrl_run_immutable`** (BR-PYRL-06) — `BEFORE UPDATE` on `pyrl_run`, once `OLD.status` has ever reached `COMMITTED`/`PAID`/`FILED`, freezes `totals`/`period_key`/`run_kind`/`journal_id` but leaves `status`/`committed_at`/`approved_by`/`version` writable (a committed run still progresses to `PAID`/`FILED`); **`trg_pyrl_run_line_immutable`** (BR-PYRL-06, line-level) — `BEFORE UPDATE` on `pyrl_run_line`, once the PARENT `pyrl_run.status` has reached `COMMITTED`+, freezes every financial/identity column except `payslip_file_id`/`paid_via`/`paid_at` (filled in after commit during the `PAID` transition). AES-256-GCM envelope-encrypts `pay_details`/`bank_name`/`branch`/`account` on write via `shared/crypto/aes-gcm.util.ts`, bit-for-bit the same `encode`/`decode` shape `SettingsService.encode()`/`.decode()` established — `encryptToBuffer(JSON.stringify(value), key).toString("base64")` stored as the jsonb column's string value; `get()`/`list()`/`search()` return these four fields REDACTED (`"***"`, mirrors `SettingsService.list()`'s `REDACTED_SECRET_VALUE`), only the separately-named `getDecrypted()` returns real plaintext — documented access-control judgement call per FR-PYRL-012.1, a Pass B controller MUST permission-gate `getDecrypted()`; `statutory-tables.service.ts` (`StatutoryTablesService` — CRUD `pyrl_statutory_table`; `findEffectiveFor(kind, periodEndDate)` wraps the repository's BR-PYRL-01 lookup and throws a named `NotFoundException` — message includes the kind and "BR-PYRL-01" — when no row is effective, THE single enforcement point every `StatutoryCalculationService` method funnels through); `statutory-calculation.service.ts` (`StatutoryCalculationService` — THE tax engine, zero hardcoded rates, entirely driven by `pyrl_statutory_table.params`; `RoundingMode.HALF_EVEN` throughout per `money.ts`'s own rounding matrix for tax-style proportional splits); `loans.service.ts` (`LoansService` + two standalone pure amortization functions). **Statutory `params` jsonb contracts** (word-for-word — Pass B's real Kenyan rate seed MUST match these exactly, documented in `statutory-calculation.service.ts`'s own interfaces): PAYE = `{ bands: { min: number; max: number | null; rate: number }[]; personalReliefMonthly: number }` (`rate` a decimal fraction, `max: null` marks the unbounded top band, bands need not be pre-sorted; `computePaye()` sums each band's taxed portion at its own rate, subtracts `personalReliefMonthly`, floors at zero); NSSF = `{ tier1: { upperLimit: number; rate: number }; tier2: { lowerLimit: number; upperLimit: number; rate: number } }` (Tier I is `0..upperLimit`, Tier II is `lowerLimit..upperLimit`; employee/employer legs are computed as independent expressions reading the SAME `tier.rate` field — both happen to be equal in the current Kenyan scheme, the calculation never literally copies one leg's result into the other); SHIF = `{ rate: number; minimumAmount?: number }` (flat percentage of gross, raised to `minimumAmount` when present and the flat-rate result would fall below it); AHL = `{ employeeRate: number; employerRate: number }` (flat percentages of gross for each leg). **Amortization formulas** (`loans.service.ts`, both `RoundingMode.HALF_UP` — "ordinary monetary rounding" per `money.ts`'s rounding matrix, not the HALF_EVEN reserved for tax splits): `generateFlatSchedule()` — equal principal per period via `Money.allocate()`'s largest-remainder method (sums to EXACTLY `principal`, no leakage); flat interest per period = `(principal × annualRate) / 12`, computed ONCE and repeated unchanged for every installment regardless of `termMonths` (the defining trait of a flat-rate loan — interest is never recalculated against a declining balance); worked example (principal=120000, rate=12%/year, term=12): every period principalDue=10000.0000, interestDue=1200.0000, totals reconcile to principal(120000.0000)+totalInterest(14400.0000)=134400.0000 exactly. `generateReducingSchedule()` — standard annuity/EMI formula `EMI = P × r × (1+r)^n / ((1+r)^n − 1)` (monthly rate `r = annualRate/12`, `EMI = P/n` when `r=0`); per period `interestDue = round(balance × r, 4dp)`, `principalDue = EMI − interestDue` (LAST period instead takes `principalDue = balance`, the exact remainder, so the schedule always reconciles to zero regardless of rounding drift); **documented float usage**: `Math.pow` computes the `(1+r)^n` scalar ONLY — the result is rounded to Money's 4dp scale immediately, at the edge, before becoming a `Money` value, the per-period loop itself is pure `Money`/bigint arithmetic; worked example (principal=120000, rate=12%/year, term=12 months, `r=0.01`): EMI=10661.8546, full 12-row schedule hand/reference-computed and asserted exactly in `loans.service.spec.ts` (seq1: interest=1200.0000/principal=9461.8546; seq2: interest=1105.3815/principal=9556.4731; …; seq12 (last): interest=105.5629/principal=10556.2923, balance→0.0000), totalPrincipal=120000.0000, totalInterest=7942.2558. `LoansService.create()` — `status='PENDING_APPROVAL'`, `balance=principal` (set at creation per the entity's own doc comment — no DB default exists), allocates a loan number via `NumberingService.allocate(em, 'PYRL_LOAN')`, submits via `ApprovalEngineService.submit(em, {domainCode:'PAYROLL_LOANS', ...})` (`PAYROLL_LOANS_APPROVAL_DOMAIN_CODE`, same day-one bootstrapping-gap caveat every other domain module's own `*_APPROVAL_DOMAIN_CODE` carries — no seed publishes a workflow def/version under it yet). `onApprovalDecided(em, loanId, approved)` — interim manual-trigger pattern (no dispatcher exists, same caveat `BudgetsService.onApprovalDecided()` documents); on reject: `status='WRITTEN_OFF'` immediately (documented choice — the 4-value enum has no `REJECTED`); on approve: `status='ACTIVE'`, generates the full schedule (FLAT or REDUCING per `rate_kind`), `due_period` anchored to the CALENDAR MONTH the decision itself is recorded in (documented judgement call — the method's brief-fixed signature takes no explicit start-period parameter). `recordRecovery()` accumulates `recovered_amount` on the matching `due_period` row and decrements `pyrl_loan.balance`, flipping to `SETTLED` at zero. `settleEarly()` zeroes `principal_due`/`interest_due` on every STRICTLY FUTURE, not-yet-recovered installment and force-settles the loan to `balance=0`/`status='SETTLED'`. **Not built in this pass, correctly deferred to Pass B**: BR-PYRL-04 mid-period-exit day-count proration (`StatutoryCalculationService`'s methods are documented to accept ALREADY-PRORATED figures, never assume a full month); BR-PYRL-03 protected-net-floor enforcement on loan recoveries (that's the RUN-computation engine's job, this pass only provides `recordRecovery()`/`settleEarly()` as building blocks); the run lifecycle itself, payslip/statutory output generation, all controllers, the permission catalogue, and the REAL Kenyan PAYE/NSSF/SHIF/AHL rate seed (this pass's tests use only SYNTHETIC params). `payroll.module.ts` now imports `SettingsModule`/`ApprovalsModule` (both already listed in `module-deps.json`'s `domains/payroll` `mayImport` from the foundation pass, now exercised for real) and provides `AppConfigService` directly (not exported by `SettingsModule`, same "each module provides its own instance" precedent `platform/files`' `FilesModule` establishes). `index.ts` barrel extended with all 8 services + their public types. 75 new tests (one spec per service, `statutory-calculation.service.spec.ts` covers all 4 tax kinds crossing every band/tier boundary plus the missing-table-throws-named-error case per kind, `loans.service.spec.ts` covers both amortization functions' worked examples plus the full `LoansService` lifecycle, both EXCLUDE-violation-to-`ConflictException` translations unit-tested with mocked `23P01`/nested-`driverError` error shapes) — 1005/1005 total passing (930 baseline + 75 new, no regressions), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint src` clean. Migration `0160-create-reporting-tables-and-mvs.ts` — Part A: the 3 `rpt_*` tables (real FKs to `usr_user`/`file_object`, `ck_rpt_schedule_format`/`ck_rpt_export_job_status` CHECKs, partial indexes `ix_rpt_schedule_active_p`/`ix_rpt_export_job_status_p` for the next pass's scheduler/worker poll queries). Part B: the 5 materialized views, each `CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW ... AS SELECT ...` (populated immediately, NOT `WITH NO DATA`, so `REFRESH ... CONCURRENTLY` works from the very first refresh) with a `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX` — **exact SQL logic and every simplification**: (1) `mv_daily_collections` — `pay_receipt` (`status='POSTED'`) joined to `pay_receipt_split`, grouped by `(receipt_date, method, cashier_id)`, `SUM(split.amount)`; **the DDL's `category` dimension was dropped** (would need `pay_receipt_allocation`→`bill_invoice_line`→fee category, excessive join complexity per the task brief's own explicit allowance — documented as aspirational, not load-bearing for the KPI). (2) `mv_ar_summary` — open (`balance>0 AND status<>'VOID'`) `bill_invoice` joined to `std_student` for `class_id`, grouped by `(class_id, aging_bucket)` where `aging_bucket` is a `CASE` on `CURRENT_DATE - due_date` (`0-30`/`31-60`/`61-90`/`90+`); **not-yet-due invoices (negative day difference) fold into the `0-30` bucket** (the DDL names no separate "current" bucket — standard aging-report convention, documented judgement call). (3) `mv_income_expense` — `gl_period_account_total` joined to `gl_account` (`class IN ('INCOME','EXPENSE')`) and `gl_period`, grouped by `(period_id, gl_account.class)` — used `class` only, not a nonexistent "group" column (the DDL's "account class/group" paraphrase has no real `group` column anywhere in the schema, per the task's own explicit narrowing); `period_starts_on`/`period_ends_on` carried through for chart labeling. Report-of-record income statements bypass this view and read `gl_journal_line` directly (FR-RPT-008). (4) `mv_wallet_liability` — **the one genuine schema limitation**: `SELECT CURRENT_DATE, COALESCE(SUM(balance),0) FROM wall_wallet` with no `GROUP BY` (always exactly one row, even when empty) — `wall_wallet` carries no historical date axis anywhere in this schema, so this is a single ALWAYS-CURRENT snapshot row keyed by `snapshot_date`, not a real time series; every refresh either updates today's row in place or (on a calendar-day rollover) `CONCURRENTLY`'s diff algorithm deletes the stale row and inserts a fresh one — **no history ever accumulates**, a real daily trend would need a separate append-only snapshot-history table this system doesn't have yet (documented on `MvWalletLiabilityRow`'s own doc comment, out of this pass's scope). (5) `mv_defaulters` — overdue (`balance>0 AND due_date<CURRENT_DATE AND status<>'VOID'`) `bill_invoice` joined to `std_student`, grouped PER STUDENT (matches the DDL's own `(student → overdue, days)` shape, not per-invoice) — `SUM(balance)` as `overdue_amount`, `MAX(CURRENT_DATE - due_date)` as `days_overdue` (the worst/oldest overdue invoice drives the headline figure). Part C — **no DB-level `fn_refresh_all_mvs()` wrapper function was built, explicitly**: the 5 views need different refresh cadences per the DDL's own table (60s/60s/5min/hourly/5min), so one monolithic SQL function refreshing all 5 in lockstep would work against that requirement rather than for it; `MaterializedViewsRepository.refresh(viewName)` is the real per-view entry point a future scheduler will call on its own timer. **View-entity/query-access approach**: plain DTO classes (`Mv*Row`), NOT TypeORM `@ViewEntity`/`@Entity` — none of the 5 views has a genuine single-column PK (each is a composite-keyed `GROUP BY` aggregate), and `dataSource.getRepository()` requires entities-array registration, which the task brief explicitly said NOT to do for these — so a `getRepository()` path was never reachable without contradiction; raw `DataSource.query()` with hand-mapped columns (incl. explicit `::text` casts on every `date`-typed column, since plain `pg` — unlike TypeORM's entity-hydration path — parses `date` into a JS `Date` object by default) keeps every mapping visible in one file. Registered in `data-source.ts`: the 3 owned entities only, NOT the 5 MV view-entities (documented inline in `data-source.ts` — they have no PK/migration lifecycle in the TypeORM sense). `module-deps.json`'s new `domains/reporting` entry is **deliberately narrow**: `mayImport: ["shared", "accounting", "platform/settings", "platform/users", "platform/files"]` — no domain module imports (`domains/payments`/`domains/billing`/`domains/students`/`domains/wallet`, all of whose tables the MVs actually read from) despite the MVs reading across them, because the MV SQL lives entirely in a raw migration file, which isn't subject to the module-boundary lint rule the way application/domain source is — a future pass extends this list only if it needs a real domain *service*, not just a raw table read. One integration test, `reporting-foundation.integration.spec.ts`: 3-table reachability plus the pass's most valuable check — a realistic cross-module fixture (student + receipt + wallet + overdue invoice + two real `PostingService.post()`-posted journals covering INCOME and EXPENSE) proving all 5 views `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY` and query correctly end-to-end against a real Postgres instance; self-skips without Docker like every other integration spec. 4 new tests, 1177/1177 total passing (1173 baseline + 4 new, no regressions — one incidental jest-worker OOM crash on the first full-suite run was an environment/parallelism artifact unrelated to this pass, resolved by re-running with `--maxWorkers=2`), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint src` clean. `list()`/`findByIdOrFail()` never decrypt `secret_enc`, only the internal `getDecryptedSecret()` `WebhookDeliveryService` calls does); `webhook-delivery.service.ts`, the core engine — `dispatch(em, eventType, payload)` fans out one `PENDING` delivery per `findActiveForEvent()` match (a directly-callable method, NOT auto-wired to any outbox event — no outbox dispatcher/worker exists anywhere in this codebase, same documented gap as everywhere else); `attemptDelivery(em, deliveryId)` builds the EXACT `X-Klickit-Signature: t=<unix-seconds>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256 of "{timestamp}.{rawBody}", keyed by the decrypted secret>` header and POSTs via `WebhookHttpClient`; `processDue(em)` is a MANUAL trigger only (`findDueForRetry(now())` + `attemptDelivery()` each, partial-failure-tolerant), no scheduler/worker exists. **Exact 8-step backoff schedule** (`WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_SCHEDULE_MINUTES`), the delay applied AFTER the Nth failed attempt, indexed `[attempt-1]`: 1min / 5min / 15min / 1h / 3h / 6h / 12h / 24h — cumulative elapsed time from the first failure through the START of the 8th (final) attempt is the sum of the first 7 deltas, 1+5+15+60+180+360+720 = 1341min ≈ 22h21m, matching FR-INTG-007.1's "retries 8× over 24 hours"; attempt 8's own failure goes straight to `DEAD` (terminal, excluded from `findDueForRetry()`'s partial index) rather than scheduling a 9th `FAILED` retry — the schedule's 8th entry (24h) is computed and stored on the DEAD row for symmetry/documentation but nothing ever queries it again. **Auto-disable mechanism** (FR-INTG-007.1, 72h): tracked on the SUBSCRIPTION (not any one delivery, since a subscription keeps accumulating failures across many dispatched deliveries even after one delivery's own 8-attempt cycle goes DEAD) via `failure_streak_started_at` — a success clears it; the FIRST failure since success sets it; every subsequent failure checks `Date.now() - failureStreakStartedAt >= 72h` and, once true, calls `WebhookSubscriptionsService.disable()` (real `is_active=false`+`disabled_reason`) plus a best-effort `platform/comms` `NotificationsService.send()` EMAIL alert to a new `AppConfigService.integrationsAdminAlertEmail` getter (`INTEGRATIONS_ADMIN_ALERT_EMAIL` env var, dev-default fallback) — a failed alert is logged and swallowed, never fails the triggering delivery attempt (a real bug was caught and fixed here: the auto-disable elapsed-time check originally used `new Date()`, which does NOT delegate to a `Date.now()` jest spy, making the 72h path untestable/inconsistent with the rest of the service's `Date.now()`-based timestamp math — fixed to use `Date.now()` throughout). `accounting-sync.service.ts` — `pushEntity()` resolves the adapter, calls it, and ALWAYS writes an `intg_sync_log` row (log-then-classify, not classify-then-maybe-log — mirrors `NotificationsService.send()`'s own "failure is a recorded state" design); `testConnection()` delegates straight to the adapter per FR-SET-003.1. `api/` — 3 controllers (`webhook-subscriptions` CRUD + rotate-secret/disable/enable, `webhook-deliveries` list/get + manual `POST :id/retry` + `POST process-due`, `sync` `POST push`/`POST test-connection`/`GET log`), full DTOs/Swagger, all 3 explicitly verified present in `integrations.module.ts`'s `controllers` array; `domains/integrations`' `WebhookHttpClient` established), `row-count-sampler.ts` (generic `QueryFn`-based table sampling — `information_schema.tables` driven, NOT a hardcoded domain table list, since this module's `mayImport` stays narrow), `backup-storage-client.ts` (a SEPARATE raw `@aws-sdk/client-s3` MinIO client for bucket-level list/mirror/get — `StoragePort` doesn't expose those, only what `FilesService` needs), `offsite-s3.adapter.ts` (optional destination, settings-driven). `application/` — `backup-encryption.util.ts` (SHA-256-derived AES-256 key from `BACKUP_PASSPHRASE`; `passphraseCheck` "key-check block" per FR-BKP-001.1, fails fast on a wrong passphrase before a full decrypt attempt), `retention.util.ts` (`classifyRetention()`, the GFS math — see algorithm below), `backup-orchestrator.service.ts` (`runBackup()`/`pruneOldBackups()`), `restore-verification.service.ts` (`verifyBackup()`/`compareRowCounts()`), `ops-health.service.ts` (`getHealthSummary()`). **GFS retention algorithm** (`retention.util.ts`'s own doc comment has the full write-up): classification is DERIVED from each run's `started_at` rather than a stored column — sort candidates DESC, then for each of 3 tiers walk the list keyed by a period function (`dayKey`=UTC calendar date, `weekKey`=a ROLLING 7-day bucket anchored at the Unix epoch — a deliberate simplification over ISO-8601 week numbers to dodge year-boundary edge cases, since this is purely an internal bucketing detail with no user-facing "week N" label, `monthKey`=UTC calendar month, deliberately NOT rolling since "last 12 calendar months" IS meaningful), keeping the FIRST (=newest) run per distinct period key until N periods are seen (7/4/12); `keepIds` = the UNION across all 3 tiers (a run can simultaneously be that day's/week's/month's representative, the classic GFS space-efficiency property) — verified in `retention.util.spec.ts` with a synthetic 20-run/40-day-spacing dataset engineered so every run lands in its own distinct day/week/month bucket, making the union collapse to EXACTLY the 12 most recent (also rules out a "sum instead of union" bug, which would incorrectly try to keep all 20). `pruneOldBackups()` only GFS-rotates `kind='SCHEDULED'` runs — `MANUAL`/`PRE_UPDATE` deliberately excluded (a manual backup or an upgrade-rollback safety net shouldn't silently vanish on the nightly-cron rotation schedule), documented on `RETENTION_MANAGED_KIND`'s own doc comment; actually deletes each pruned run's destination files/objects (best-effort per destination, same "orphaned blob beats losing prune progress" rationale `FilesService.upload()`'s own compensating-delete already established) AND the `bkp_backup_run` row. `runBackup()`'s whole flow (`pg_dump` → files-bucket mirror → `.env` snapshot → tar → AES-256-GCM encrypt → SHA-256 manifest → LOCAL/MINIO/OFFSITE_S3 fan-out → row-count manifest capture) is wrapped in one try/catch/finally so the run row is ALWAYS left `'OK'`/`'FAILED'`, never stuck `'RUNNING'` — LOCAL destination failure propagates (critical, primary copy), MINIO/OFFSITE_S3 failures are recorded per-destination (`ok:false`/`error`) without failing the overall run. `api/` — `backups.controller.ts` (`POST /backups/run`, `GET /backups`, `GET /backups/:id`, `POST /backups/:id/verify-restore`, `POST /backups/prune`), `ops.controller.ts` (`GET /ops/health`), full Swagger, `dto/` folder, both controllers explicitly verified present in `backups-ops.module.ts`'s `controllers` array, route prefixes `backups`/`ops` checked against every other module's own prefix (no collision, Module 18's own lesson). 5 new permission codes appended (`backups:run:create/view`, `backups:restore:verify`, `backups:retention:prune`, `ops:health:view`) — `0900` seed needed ZERO code changes (same confirmed-no-op precedent Module 19 established) and no workflow/GL-account seeds (no approval chains, no GL posting). **Cross-module grants**: `platform/files`' `files.module.ts` now exports `STORAGE_PORT` alongside `FilesService` (a NEW `exports` entry — the barrel already exported the TS-level `STORAGE_PORT` symbol/type, but that alone doesn't make the DI *provider* resolvable cross-module, the same "barrel exports the shape, the runtime grant still needs an explicit `exports` entry" distinction every prior cross-module grant in this codebase has hit) offsite-S3 destination's runtime-configurable credentials (`backups.offsite_s3.*` keys, `isSecret=true` for the key pair) — a deliberate choice over minting a new `SetIntegrationKind` enum member on "Database" group — the first APPLICATION-code, not just migration-`data-source.ts`, consumer of those env vars). `module-deps.json`'s new `domains/backups-ops` entry: `mayImport: ["shared", "platform/settings", "platform/files", "platform/users"]` (per the task brief's literal list; `platform/users` listed for parity only — no `bkp_*` table takes a real FK to `usr_user`). completely separate from the global `APP_GUARD` pipeline), `license-api.controller.ts` (`/license/v1/*`, all 9 endpoints, every response returned as `{token: <signed JWS>}` — no parallel unsigned body, matching §2.6's "portal verifies the instance's signature" literally), `license-status.controller.ts` (staff-facing `GET /license/status`\|`/license/api-log`\|`/license/update-notices`, normal JWT pipeline + one new permission code `license:status:view`, deliberately NOT `@ExemptFromLicenseGuard()` — see below). **The central architectural achievement — `license.v_usage_stats`/`license.v_state` symmetric isolation-preserving views** (migration `0190-create-license-tables-and-views.ts`, read that file's own extensive doc comment for the full write-up): `kfe_app` has zero grants on `license.*` and `kfe_license` has zero grants on `app.*`, yet `LicenseStateGuard` (shared/rbac) needs the current license state and `UsageStatsViewRepository` needs 4 cross-schema figures (`active_users_30d` from `usr_login_event`, `student_count` from `std_student`, `storage_bytes` from `file_object`, `last_backup_at` from `bkp_backup_run`). Resolved with two narrow, purpose-built, read-only VIEWS, mirror images of each other: `license.v_state` (single-row over `license.license`, `GRANT SELECT` + `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA license` to `kfe_app`) and `license.v_usage_stats` (single-row aggregate over the 4 `app.*` tables, `GRANT SELECT` to `kfe_license`, redundant with but explicit alongside migration `0002`'s existing `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` rule). The mechanism works because Postgres checks a view's underlying-table access against the VIEW OWNER's privileges (`kfe_migrate`, who owns every table via having created them) — the querying role never needs a direct grant on the underlying tables, only `SELECT` on the view. A view's DEFINITION SQL crosses no TypeScript import boundary at all (only `import` statements are ESLint-checked), so building it in a migration doesn't violate the module's `mayImport:["shared"]` restriction. **Honestly documented, real-vs-test environment gap**: this codebase currently connects with ONE shared DB role for the whole app (no separate `kfe_app`/`kfe_license` connection pools exist anywhere in the code) — the grants/views built here are correct, real Postgres DDL that enforces the isolation in PRODUCTION once per-role connections are wired up; they do not (and cannot) change which SQL an in-process `DataSource` can run in THIS test environment. The real, CI-enforced boundary in this environment is the TypeScript import graph. **`LicenseStateGuard` wired into the global pipeline, closing Module 1's own flagged gap** ("LicenseGuard omitted from the guard pipeline for now, licensing is module 21", `docs/phase-5/00-module-plan.md`): built at `shared/rbac/license-state.guard.ts` (NOT inside `licensing/`, since nothing may import that module — it queries `license.v_state` via raw `DataSource.query()`, a short 5s process-local cache to avoid a DB round trip per request), registered in `platform/auth/auth.module.ts`'s `APP_GUARD` list in the documented `Jwt -> License -> Permissions -> Authority` order. New `@ExemptFromLicenseGuard()` decorator at `shared/rbac/exempt-from-license-guard.decorator.ts` (mirrors `@Public()`'s exact shape), applied to: `platform/auth/api/auth.controller.ts`'s `login`/`2fa/verify`/`refresh`/`logout`/`otp/request`/`otp/verify` handlers; `domains/reporting/api/export-jobs.controller.ts` (class-level, both routes); `domains/backups-ops/api/backups.controller.ts` (class-level, all 5 routes). New exceptions: `shared/exceptions/license-suspended.exception.ts` (`LicenseSuspendedException`/`LicenseDeactivatedException`, own `code` strings so the API envelope literally reads `LICENSE_SUSPENDED`/`LICENSE_DEACTIVATED` per FR-LIC-006.1's own wording, rather than reusing `AuthorizationException`'s fixed `"FORBIDDEN"` code). **Guard semantics, a documented judgement call**: SUSPENDED blocks non-GET on non-exempt endpoints (BR-LIC-01: reads/exports/backups never blocked); DEACTIVATED blocks EVERY non-exempt endpoint regardless of method (FR-LIC-006.1's literal wording — "login restricted to System Admin with export/backup screens only" is read as MORE restrictive than BR-LIC-01's general "never blocks reads" principle, a deliberate narrower carve-out for this one state) — the finer-grained "restricted to System Admin" ROLE check on who may complete login while DEACTIVATED is NOT implemented (would need `AuthService.login()` itself to branch on license state + caller role, outside this pass's "add a decorator to route handlers" scope); `EXPIRED` (reachable via no code path in this pass — see below) is treated at least as restrictively as `SUSPENDED`. **Config**: `AppConfigService` gained `schoolId`, `infoneyLicensePublicKeyCurrent`/`Previous` (JSON `{kid,publicKey}`, same rotation shape as `jwtKeyCurrent`/`Previous`), `instancePrivateKeyPem`, `licenseFilePath` — real Ed25519 dev-default keypairs generated once via `crypto.generateKeyPairSync('ed25519')` for this pass (public-key-only for Infoney's side — its real private key never lives here); `.env.example` extended with `INFONEY_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY_CURRENT/PREVIOUS`, `INSTANCE_PRIVATE_KEY`, `LICENSE_FILE_PATH`. One new permission code, `license:status:view`, appended to `platform/users/domain/permission-catalogue.ts`.
