| — | Full Phase 5 verification | ✅ **COMPLETE, verified against a real live Postgres 16 instance** — see "Phase 5 Full Verification" section below for the full write-up. |

## Phase 5 Full Verification — 2026-07-22

**Verdict: Phase 5 backend development — all 21 modules — is now fully verified against a real, live PostgreSQL 16 instance. Schema, triggers, constraints, and application logic have all been proven to actually work, not just compile and pass mocked unit tests.** This is the first time in the entire build that any of this codebase's 190+ migrations or integration tests ever touched a real database — every prior "green" test run was 1372/1372 self-skipping integration specs plus real unit tests. This section closes that gap.

**Environment**: Docker Desktop + WSL2 installed by the user; `docker-compose.dev.yml` (postgres:16 + redis:7) brought up healthy; root `.env` created from `.env.example` pointing at the containers.

### Migration chain
All 190+ migrations ran cleanly end-to-end on a completely fresh database (162 tables across `app`/`license`/`audit` schemas, ending with the `0900` seed). **One environmental finding, not a migration bug**: TypeORM's own internal migration-bookkeeping table creation (`app.typeorm_migrations`) runs *before* migration `0001` and requires its configured default schema (`schema: "app"` in `data-source.ts`) to already exist — so a truly from-zero database (zero schemas, not just empty tables) needs `CREATE SCHEMA app; CREATE SCHEMA license; CREATE SCHEMA audit;` as a one-line bootstrap step before the very first `migration:run`. This is exactly the kind of step a real installer script would perform anyway (per `docs/phase-3/03-deployment-infrastructure.md`'s guided-installer flow, "pull & up" precedes "migrations"); worth a one-line callout in that installer's implementation whenever Phase 8 builds it, but not a defect in any migration file itself.

**M-4 gate** (`docs/phase-4/01-standards-and-migrations.md`'s CI rule: fresh DB → `migration:run` all → `migration:revert` last → `migration:run` again): passed cleanly on a genuinely fresh, unpopulated database. (First attempt was run against a database that already had integration-test-generated `appr_instance` rows referencing the seeded workflow versions, which correctly failed the revert with a real FK violation — that was a sequencing mistake in the verification process itself, not a schema bug; re-run against a truly fresh DB round-tripped without error.)

### License schema isolation — spot-checked directly via `psql`, not just trusted
- `kfe_license` grants on `app.*`: **0 rows** (queried `information_schema.role_table_grants`).
- `kfe_app` grants on `license.*`: **exactly 1 row** — `SELECT` on `license.v_state`, nothing else.
- `kfe_license`'s own grants: full DML on its 4 owned tables plus `SELECT`-reachable `v_state`/`v_usage_stats` views (expected — it owns its schema; the `v_usage_stats` view's ability to read `app.*` aggregates works via the view-OWNER's (`kfe_migrate`) privileges, never a direct grant to `kfe_license` on any `app.*` table).
This confirms Module 21's whole architectural premise — the mirror-image `license.v_state`/`license.v_usage_stats` views — actually enforces the isolation at the database level, not just in the TypeScript import graph.

### Trigger/constraint correctness
Every safety-critical trigger across all 21 modules was exercised for real via the integration test suite (not spot-checked separately — the suite's own trigger-integration specs, one per module with DB-enforced invariants, ARE the direct check): `trg_gl_journal_balanced`, `trg_gl_writer_guard`, `trg_gl_journal_immutable`, `trg_gl_account_deactivation_only`, the `pyrl_employee_assignment`/`pyrl_employee_component` `EXCLUDE USING gist` no-overlap constraints, `trg_bill_installments_sum`, `trg_pay_splits_sum`/`trg_pay_allocations_sum`, and every other module's own immutability/sum-check triggers all correctly accept valid data and reject invalid data under real Postgres enforcement.

### Real bugs found and fixed by this live-database run (unit tests with mocked repos could never have caught these)
1. **`NumberingService.createSeriesOnFirstUse()` — transaction-poisoning bug** (`platform/settings/application/numbering.service.ts`). The "insert, catch `23505`, fall back to a locked read" pattern didn't account for a hard Postgres rule: **any error inside a transaction poisons the whole transaction until a `ROLLBACK`**, even one caught in application code — so the fallback read after a caught unique-violation failed with `25P02 current transaction is aborted`. Fixed with a `SAVEPOINT` taken before the risky insert, `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` on failure, so the fallback read runs inside the same transaction correctly. (`repo.manager?.queryRunner` guarded with optional chaining so the unit tests' mocked `EntityManager`, which has no `.manager`, still passes.)
2. **`runInTransaction()`'s retry-on-`40001` bound was too tight for its own stress test** (`shared/database/tx.ts`). This retry policy was itself a fix made mid-verification (see below) — its first tuning (8 attempts) could still occasionally exhaust itself against `settings.integration.spec.ts`'s deliberately adversarial 10-way-simultaneous stress test through sheer thundering-herd bad luck (not a logic bug — Postgres's own serializable-snapshot-isolation semantics under extreme, unrealistic contention). Bumped to 15, confirmed stable across multiple consecutive full-suite runs.
3. **`NumberingService.allocate()` had no retry policy for genuine Postgres `40001` (serialization_failure) under real concurrent load at all**, even though this exact gap was flagged as necessary back when Module 2 built the allocator (see that module's own PROGRESS.md entry) and never actually implemented. Fixed generically at the transaction boundary in `runInTransaction()` (`shared/database/tx.ts`) rather than inside `allocate()` itself — a `40001` means the enclosing transaction is already dead, so only re-running the *entire* `work` callback in a brand-new transaction can recover; `runInTransaction()` is the sole choke point every real caller in this codebase transacts through (confirmed via grep — no application code calls `dataSource.transaction()` directly), so fixing it there covers `PostingService.post()`, `NumberingService.allocate()`, and every other caller uniformly with no per-service opt-in.
4. **Several e2e capstone tests' cleanup code tried to delete `gl_period`/`gl_fiscal_year`/`gl_account` rows that real postings during the test had made permanently referenced by immutable `gl_journal_line` rows** (`wallet-e2e`, `payroll-e2e`, `billing-e2e`, `expenses-e2e` integration specs) — a real FK violation, not a product bug. `payments-e2e.integration.spec.ts` had already established the correct pattern (leave them as inert, uniquely-suffixed residue with an explanatory comment, since `gl_journal_line` can never be deleted by design); the other four just hadn't followed it. Fixed all four to match.
5. One stale test assertion (`tx.spec.ts` expected the old attempt-count constant, not updated when the constant was tuned) and one genuinely test-only phone-number-uniqueness collision — both trivial, fixed.

### Final live-database test results
`pnpm --filter server test -- --maxWorkers=2` — **1379/1379 tests passing, 174/174 suites**, confirmed stable across multiple consecutive full runs (not a lucky single pass). `tsc --noEmit` clean. `eslint src` clean (0 errors; 5 pre-existing, unrelated `no-unused-vars` warnings in test-cleanup residue code). Every integration spec across all 21 modules now genuinely executes against the live database instead of self-skipping.

### `apps/api` status
**Does not exist anywhere in this repo.** Confirmed via `Glob apps/**` → no results. This is expected and was never in scope for any of the 21 module-build passes — every module was built as an importable NestJS library module under `packages/server/src/{platform,domains,accounting,licensing}`, ready for a future composition root, not yet assembled into one. Given this, a genuine HTTP-level smoke test (login → CRUD → Swagger) is not currently possible. The closest honest equivalent is what the integration test suite already does: real service instances, real `DataSource` connections, real multi-step business lifecycles (create → submit → approve → post → verify-GL-balance) exercised end-to-end against the live database, across nearly every module. **Assembling `apps/api` (and `apps/worker`) is the actual next prerequisite before any real HTTP-level testing, local dev server usage, or deployment — distinct from and additional to Phase 5's module-building scope, and worth flagging explicitly before Phase 6 (frontend) work assumes a running backend to call.**

## apps/api Composition Root — 2026-07-22

**Verdict: `apps/api` now exists, boots, and serves real HTTP traffic against the live PostgreSQL 16 + Redis 7 containers.** All 22 module classes (Module 1 contributes two — `AuthModule`+`UsersModule`) are composed into one running `AppModule`, verified live: 509 routes registered across every module, `/health`+`/health/ready` both green with real DB/Redis probes, Swagger serving a full OpenAPI document, and a genuine login → JWT → authenticated-request round trip proven end-to-end. This closes the gap the "Phase 5 Full Verification" section above flagged as the actual next prerequisite before Phase 6 (frontend) work assumes a running backend.

### `packages/server` barrel (`src/index.ts`) — new file
`@klickit/server`'s `package.json` had declared `"main": "dist/index.js"`/`"types": "dist/index.d.ts"` since it was first created, but no file ever backed those paths — every module was importable only via its own deep relative path, never as one package. Rather than have `apps/api` reach across the workspace boundary with `../../packages/server/src/...` relative imports (which would have dragged `packages/server`'s files into `apps/api`'s own `tsc` `rootDir`, a real TS6059 error), a small barrel (`packages/server/src/index.ts`) now re-exports exactly what a composition root needs: the 22 module classes, `AppDataSource` (so `TypeOrmModule.forRoot()` reuses its `entities`/`namingStrategy` verbatim — `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` reads `AppDataSource.options.entities`/`.namingStrategy`, never calls `.initialize()` on it, and supplies its own APP-role (`kfe_app`) credentials, never `DB_MIGRATION_USER`/`kfe_migrate`, per M-5), `AllExceptionsFilter`, and the handful of shared-kernel symbols `health.controller.ts` needs (`REDIS_CLIENT`, `Public`, `ExemptFromLicenseGuard`). `pnpm --filter @klickit/server run build` now must run before `apps/api` can resolve `@klickit/server` at runtime (Node reads `dist/index.js` via the pnpm workspace symlink) — `turbo.json`'s existing generic `"build": {"dependsOn": ["^build"]}` task already expresses this dependency automatically once `apps/api/package.json` declares `@klickit/server: "workspace:*"`, no `turbo.json` edit was needed.

### Shared-infra consolidation — investigated, and done
Grepped every `*.module.ts` for local `AppConfigService`/`redisClientProvider` provider declarations before composing anything, per this task's own "investigate, don't just assume" instruction. Found: **6 modules** (`platform/auth`, `licensing`, `domains/backups-ops`, `platform/files`, `domains/payroll`, `platform/settings`) each independently declared their own local `AppConfigService` provider; **4 modules** (`platform/auth`, `licensing`, `domains/backups-ops`, `domains/reporting`) each independently declared their own local `redisClientProvider` — a real `FactoryProvider` that opens a brand-new `ioredis` TCP connection (`lazyConnect: false`) per declaration. Union: 7 distinct module files affected — well past the "more than a couple" threshold the task brief named as the trigger for consolidation.

Built `packages/server/src/shared/infra/shared-infra.module.ts` — a `@Global()` `SharedInfraModule` providing `AppConfigService`+`redisClientProvider` exactly once, imported only at the `apps/api` root (`app.module.ts`). It lives under `shared/` (not `apps/api/`) specifically so `licensing` — which per `module-deps.json` may import `shared` ONLY (`"mayImport": ["shared"]`, `"importableBy": []`) — stays consistent with its own isolation rule; that was verified first (no new import edge leaves `shared`), then licensing's own local registration was removed like the other 6, confirmed via `Test-Path`/re-read after editing. Every one of the 7 affected module files had only its local provider-array entry deleted (import statements kept where still needed for a factory's `inject:` array, e.g. `files.module.ts`'s `MulterModule.registerAsync`) — no service/guard/controller file was touched, and every existing `@Inject(REDIS_CLIENT)`/constructor `AppConfigService` injection resolves identically, now from one shared singleton instead of up to 6 duplicates. `pnpm --filter server exec tsc --noEmit` stayed clean after every edit.

### Real integration issues found composing 22 modules together
Composing all 22 for the first time surfaced two real gaps, both fixed:
1. **`@klickit/server` had no importable barrel** (see above) — would have made `apps/api` unable to depend on it as a normal package at all. Fixed by adding `src/index.ts`.
2. **`AppConfigService` had no `port`/`nodeEnv` getters** — `main.api.ts` needs both (listen port, dev-vs-prod CORS/Swagger behaviour) and every other runtime config value in this codebase already flows through `AppConfigService`'s getter-with-fallback-default pattern; adding two getters (`port` default `3000`, `nodeEnv` default `"development"`, matching `.env.example`) kept that convention intact rather than reading `process.env.PORT` directly in `main.api.ts`.

No provider-resolution errors, no duplicate-registration crashes, and no missing-`exports` failures surfaced at Nest bootstrap time beyond what the shared-infra consolidation above pre-empted — the 21 modules' own extensive `mayImport`/barrel-export discipline (documented at length in `module-deps.json`, verified by reading all 22 `*.module.ts` files before writing `app.module.ts`) meant the composition itself was mechanically clean. This is a direct dividend of the module-boundary discipline enforced throughout Phase 5, not luck.

### Files built
- `packages/server/src/index.ts` — new barrel (see above).
- `packages/server/src/shared/infra/shared-infra.module.ts` — new `SharedInfraModule`.
- `packages/server/src/shared/config/app-config.service.ts` — added `port`/`nodeEnv` getters.
- 7 edited module files (local `AppConfigService`/`redisClientProvider` provider entries removed): `platform/auth/auth.module.ts`, `licensing/licensing.module.ts`, `domains/backups-ops/backups-ops.module.ts`, `platform/files/files.module.ts`, `domains/payroll/payroll.module.ts`, `platform/settings/settings.module.ts`, `domains/reporting/reporting.module.ts`.
- `apps/api/package.json` — `"@klickit/api"`, depends on `@klickit/server: "workspace:*"` plus the same pinned `@nestjs/*`/`typeorm`/`class-validator`/`class-transformer`/`ioredis` versions `packages/server/package.json` already uses (no second version of anything introduced). `start:dev` runs `ts-node src/main.api.ts` directly — `packages/server` itself has no dev-watch tool (`ts-node-dev`/`nodemon`) anywhere in this codebase, only plain `ts-node` (via `typeorm-ts-node-commonjs` for migrations), so `start:dev` matches that, not a newly-introduced tool.
- `apps/api/tsconfig.json` — extends `packages/config/tsconfig/nestjs.json`, same pattern `packages/server/tsconfig.json` uses.
- `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` — imports all 22 modules + root `TypeOrmModule.forRoot()` (reusing `AppDataSource.options.entities`/`.namingStrategy`, APP-role credentials) + global `APP_FILTER` (`AllExceptionsFilter`) + global `APP_PIPE` (`ValidationPipe({ whitelist: true, transform: true })`) + `SharedInfraModule`.
- `apps/api/src/health.controller.ts` — `GET /health` (bare liveness) + `GET /health/ready` (real `SELECT 1` + real Redis `PING`; MinIO reported `"skipped"`, never hard-failed, since it isn't part of this dev environment). Both `@Public()`+`@ExemptFromLicenseGuard()`. Deliberately distinct from `domains/backups-ops`'s existing `GET /ops/health` (kept, unmerged, as instructed).
- `apps/api/src/main.api.ts` — `NestFactory.create(AppModule)`, global prefix `/api/v1` excluding bare `/health`+`/health/ready`, permissive dev CORS (documented production caveat), Swagger at `/api/docs` with bearer-auth scheme, listens on `AppConfigService.port`.

### Live verification results — real commands, real output
Booted via `pnpm --filter api run start:dev` (background process, stdout/stderr redirected to files, waited for the Nest startup log line) against the already-running `klickiterp-postgres-1`/`klickiterp-redis-1` containers and the real root `.env`. Startup log ended with:
```
[Nest] 31916 - ... LOG [LicenseFileService] No license file at ./data/license/license.json — instance not yet registered.
[Nest] 31916 - ... LOG [NestApplication] Nest application successfully started +2ms
[Nest] 31916 - ... LOG [Bootstrap] Klickit Finance ERP API listening on port 3000 (env: development)
[Nest] 31916 - ... LOG [Bootstrap] Swagger docs: http://localhost:3000/api/docs
```
(The `LicenseFileService` line is expected — no license file has ever been provisioned in this environment, see Module 21's own documented gap; `LicenseStateGuard` fails open with no `license.v_state` row, exactly as designed.)

1. **`GET /health`** → `200`, body `{"status":"ok","timestamp":"2026-07-28T06:37:24.950Z"}`.
2. **`GET /health/ready`** → `200`, body `{"status":"ok","checks":{"database":{"status":"ok"},"redis":{"status":"ok"},"minio":{"status":"skipped","detail":"MinIO not provisioned in this environment"}}}` — real `SELECT 1` and real Redis `PING` both executed against the live containers.
3. **`GET /api/docs-json`** → `200`, OpenAPI document with **509 paths**, spanning every module (`/api/v1/auth/*`, `/api/v1/billing/*`, `/api/v1/payments/*`, `/api/v1/payroll/*`, `/api/v1/wallet/*`, `/api/v1/banking/*`, `/api/v1/fixed-assets/*`, `/api/v1/license/*`, `/api/v1/ops/health`, `/api/v1/backups/*`, etc.) — direct proof the whole 22-module composition wired up, not just one module.
4. **`POST /api/v1/auth/login`** with wrong credentials → `401`, body `{"error":{"code":"UNAUTHENTICATED","message":"Invalid credentials"},"request_id":"019fa771-c697-7f96-8a59-7c593112d853"}` — a clean `FR-API-005.1` error envelope, not a 500; proves `AllExceptionsFilter`/`DomainException`→HTTP mapping is live.
5. **Real login → JWT → authenticated-request round trip.** `0900-seed-permissions-and-roles.ts` seeds the "System Admin" `usr_role` row and every permission grant, but no real `usr_user` (per that migration's own doc comment, provisioning a real account is an installer/bootstrap step this codebase doesn't have yet — confirmed, out of scope to build now). Created ONE throwaway dev fixture directly via raw SQL: a `usr_user` row (`username: devadmin`, bcrypt-hashed password, `status: ACTIVE`) plus a `usr_user_role` row linking it to the seeded System Admin role id. **This is a throwaway dev-only fixture, not a real installer flow — clearly not production data, created by hand for this verification pass only.** (**Superseded 2026-07-28**: the `devadmin` fixture and its `usr_session`/`usr_login_event`/`usr_user_role` rows were deleted once a real bootstrap tool existed to provision a proper account instead — see the "Installer Bootstrap (System Admin provisioning)" section below. It no longer exists in this environment's database.)
   - `POST /api/v1/auth/login {"identifier":"devadmin","password":"Klickit#DevAdmin123"}` → `201`, body includes a real ES256-signed `accessToken`, a real opaque `refreshToken`, and `"user":{"...","roles":["System Admin"]}`.
   - `GET /api/v1/users` with `Authorization: Bearer <accessToken>` → `200`, real user list data returned — proving `JwtAuthGuard` (signature+expiry+Redis session check) → `LicenseStateGuard` (fail-open, no license row) → `PermissionsGuard` (Redis-cached permission set resolved from the System Admin role, real grant check passed) → `AuthorityGuard` (no-op, no `amount` field) → `UsersController` → `UsersService` all executed for real, end-to-end, against the live database and live Redis.
6. **Process shutdown verified clean**: killed the full process tree (`ts-node` child + `pnpm` wrapper), then confirmed via `Get-Process`/`Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 3000` that no node process and no port-3000 listener remained, and a follow-up `GET /health` timed out as expected (connection refused).

One escalation worth recording for future readers: the first attempt to seed the throwaway `devadmin` password hash via `docker exec psql -c "...\$2b\$12\$..."` silently corrupted the bcrypt hash (PowerShell → `docker exec` → `psql` triple-layer shell escaping mangled the `$`-delimited bcrypt format down to three backslashes) — login failed with a generic `401` that looked identical to a real credentials error. Diagnosed by reading the stored value back (`SELECT password_hash`) rather than assuming the app was at fault; fixed by writing the `UPDATE` statement to a `.sql` file and piping it via `Get-Content -Raw | docker exec -i psql` (stdin, no shell-escaping layers at all). Not a defect in any application code — a real lesson about multi-layer shell quoting on Windows when seeding data by hand.

### What's now possible vs. still not built
**Now possible**: a real, running, HTTP-callable Klickit Finance ERP backend — `pnpm --filter api run start:dev` (or `build`+`start` for a compiled run) boots one process serving all 22 modules' REST surface under `/api/v1`, a full Swagger/OpenAPI document at `/api/docs`, and load-balancer-grade health probes at `/health`+`/health/ready`, all against the real Postgres/Redis this environment already runs. Phase 6 (frontend) work can now assume a running backend to call.

**Still not built** (unchanged from this task's own explicit scope boundary, restated here so it isn't lost): `apps/worker` (the BullMQ/cron runtime — `main.worker.ts`, ADR-003's other half); `packages/contracts` (OpenAPI-derived TS types + zod schemas for `apps/web`); a real license-file provisioning flow on first run. **The System-Admin-provisioning half of this gap is now closed** — see "Installer Bootstrap (System Admin provisioning)" below, which replaced the `devadmin` hand-seeded stand-in with a real tool. **`apps/worker` is now also closed** — see the "apps/worker — Composition Root + Outbox Dispatcher" section below (ADR-003's other half now exists, boots, and its own new real infrastructure — a generic outbox dispatcher — is live-verified; `packages/contracts` and first-run license provisioning remain the only items in this list still genuinely unbuilt).

## Installer Bootstrap (System Admin provisioning) — 2026-07-28

**Verdict: `tools/bootstrap-admin.ts` now exists — a one-shot CLI that provisions a real System Admin account (forced password change + 2FA enrollment), fully verified end-to-end against the live Postgres/Redis containers.** This is ONLY the "bootstrap: create System Admin" sub-step `docs/phase-3/03-deployment-infrastructure.md` §4.1 describes as step 5 of a full guided installer — not the OS-level installer itself (`install.sh`/`install.ps1`: preflight, TLS, compose orchestration). That whole installer is Phase 9 deployment-infrastructure scope; this codebase is still Phase 5 (backend) and has no `apps/web`/prod compose/Nginx yet. Confirmed, explicit scope decision — not re-litigated here.

### Why real services, not raw SQL
The prior "apps/api Composition Root" pass (above) proved a login round trip by hand-seeding a throwaway `devadmin` row via raw SQL — documented at the time as a stand-in, not a substitute for a real flow. This pass builds that real flow by reusing the codebase's own already-tested, DI-wired application services instead of reimplementing any of their logic:
- `UsersService.create()` — real uniqueness validation, real secure-random temp password generation, real bcrypt hashing (12 rounds), real `INVITED`/`mustChangePassword: true`/`twofaEnabled: false` row insert.
- `RolesService.assignRoleToUser()` — attaches the migration-`0900`-seeded "System Admin" role (SoD-checked, idempotent).
- `PasswordService.changePassword()` — real change-password flow, flips `mustChangePassword` to `false`.
- `TwoFactorService.enroll()`/`.activateEnroll()` — real TOTP secret generation (`otplib`), real code verification, real recovery-code issuance.
- `AuthService.login()` — real login round trip at every stage, including the `twofaEnabled` pre-auth-token branch.

No new auth/crypto/session logic was written anywhere in `packages/server` for this pass — only two small, narrowly-scoped additions to already-exported services (`UsersService.findByUsername()`, `RolesService.listUserIdsForRole()`, both one-line wrappers around existing repository methods) and a documented, narrow barrel export addition (`packages/server/src/index.ts` now also exports `UsersService`/`RolesService`/`AuthService`/`PasswordService`/`TwoFactorService` — services only, repositories still never leave their module — see that file's own doc comment for the full rationale). See `tools/README.md` for the full command reference.

### `tools/` becomes a real workspace package
`tools/` (`@klickit/tools`) was added to `pnpm-workspace.yaml` — the first real content ever placed there (previously just a layout comment in the architecture doc: "installers, backup scripts, seeders, fixtures"). `bootstrap-admin.ts` runs as `NestFactory.createApplicationContext(AppModule)` (no HTTP listener — a one-shot admin tool, not a server), reusing `apps/api/src/app.module.ts`'s own `AppModule` verbatim via a relative import (`apps/api` has no exported library surface, only `dist/main.api.js`) rather than assembling a slimmer bespoke module — no new module-wiring surface to get right, and it was already proven to boot cleanly (see "apps/api Composition Root" above). `tools/tsconfig.json` sets `rootDir` to the monorepo root specifically so this cross-package relative import type-checks cleanly — verified with a real `tsc --noEmit` run (`pnpm --filter tools run typecheck`), not just `ts-node --transpile-only`, confirming it doesn't hit the same `TS6059` "not under rootDir" error the composition-root pass documented and avoided a different way (a package barrel) for `packages/server` itself. `dotenv` loads explicitly BEFORE the `AppModule` import line (verified empirically that TypeScript's CommonJS output does NOT hoist `import` declarations above interspersed statements — an `import` after a plain statement genuinely runs after it), since `app.module.ts`'s `TypeOrmModule.forRoot()` reads `process.env.DB_HOST`/etc. synchronously at module-evaluation time.

Three commands, no interactive prompts anywhere (flags only — this environment's shell is non-interactive, a `readline` prompt would hang or read EOF immediately): `create --username <u> --full-name "<name>" [--email <e>] [--phone <p>] [--force]`, `enroll-2fa --username <u>`, `verify-2fa --username <u> --code <totp>`. `create` refuses to mint a second System Admin unless `--force` — it checks for ANY user holding the role, any status, not just `ACTIVE` (`RolesService.listUserIdsForRole()`), which is exactly what caught the pre-existing `devadmin` fixture on the first real run (see below).

### `devadmin` cleanup
Before the first real `create` could succeed, it correctly refused: `Refusing to create a second System Admin: 1 user(s) already hold the "System Admin" role (user id(s): 0fea327b-b081-4284-acdc-a27e56b0f80f)`. Deleted the superseded fixture from the live dev DB (mind the shell-escaping lesson the earlier pass already documented — piped SQL via a file/stdin, not inlined through nested PowerShell/`docker exec`/`psql` quoting): `usr_session` (1 row) and `usr_login_event` (2 rows) referencing `devadmin`'s id first (real FK violations otherwise — `usr_password_history` had 0 rows, no other module's tables referenced it), then `usr_user_role`, then `usr_user` itself. Confirmed zero rows remain: `SELECT count(*) FROM app.usr_user WHERE username = 'devadmin'` → `0`.

### Live verification results — real commands, real output
All of this ran against the already-running `klickiterp-postgres-1`/`klickiterp-redis-1` containers and the real root `.env`; every step below is an actual `bootstrap-admin.ts`/service-call result, not a prediction.

1. **`create --username bootstrap.admin --full-name "Bootstrap Admin" --email bootstrap.admin@klickit.local`** →
   ```
   System Admin account created.
     id:               019fa7aa-899b-723d-a778-8c7d3684675b
     username:         bootstrap.admin
     status:           INVITED
     mustChangePassword: true
     twofaEnabled:     false
     role:             System Admin
   ================================================================
     TEMPORARY PASSWORD — shown once, will not be displayed again
   ================================================================
     uASkcvdaDc9YjCITRUOTwdXoIrjEbJUk
   ================================================================
   ```
   A second `create` (different username, no `--force`) correctly failed with the safety-check error above, now naming `bootstrap.admin`'s own id as the existing holder — proving the guard re-checks live state, not a cached assumption.
2. **Real login with the temporary password** (`AuthService.login("bootstrap.admin", "uASkcvdaDc9YjCITRUOTwdXoIrjEbJUk", ip, ua)`) → `stage: "complete"`, real ES256 `accessToken` + opaque `refreshToken`, **`mustChangePassword: true`**, `user.roles: ["System Admin"]`.
3. **Real password change** (`PasswordService.changePassword(userId, tempPassword, "Klickit#Bootstrap2026!")`) → re-fetched the user via `UsersService.findByIdOrFail()`: **`mustChangePassword: false`**.
4. **`enroll-2fa --username bootstrap.admin`** → real `otpauthUri`/`manualKey` (`otpauth://totp/Klickit%20Finance%20ERP:bootstrap.admin?secret=OEEFUTA4EVEC6MRJ&period=30&digits=6&algorithm=SHA1&issuer=Klickit%20Finance%20ERP`). A real TOTP code was generated from `manualKey` via `otplib`'s `authenticator.generate()` (no human scanning a QR code) and fed to **`verify-2fa --username bootstrap.admin --code <that code>`** → `2FA activated for "bootstrap.admin".` plus 8 one-time recovery codes printed under the "shown once" banner. Re-fetched the user: **`twofaEnabled: true`**.
5. **Second real login with the NEW password** → `stage: "2fa"`, a real `preauthToken`, no tokens yet (the pre-auth branch, `user.twofaEnabled` now true) — completed with a freshly-generated real TOTP code via `TwoFactorService.verify(preauthToken, code)` → `stage: "complete"`, a real ES256 `accessToken`, **`mustChangePassword: false`**, `user.roles: ["System Admin"]`. Full round trip proven twice over (once via direct service calls exercising every method signature exactly, once again via the CLI's own `enroll-2fa`/`verify-2fa` commands specifically, using a second freshly-generated secret/code pair, to prove the shipped CLI surface itself — not just the underlying services — works).
6. **`devadmin` cleanup confirmed**: `SELECT count(*) FROM app.usr_user WHERE username = 'devadmin'` → `0` rows, both before finishing this pass and re-confirmed after.

`pnpm --filter server exec tsc --noEmit` clean. `pnpm --filter server exec eslint src` → 0 errors, the same 5 pre-existing unrelated `no-unused-vars` warnings the "Phase 5 Full Verification" section already documented (no new ones introduced). `pnpm --filter tools run typecheck` (`tsc --noEmit` against `tools/tsconfig.json`) clean.

### Honest gap, flagged plainly for whoever picks up runtime enforcement later
`mustChangePassword`/`twofaEnabled` remain **advisory-only fields** surfaced in the login response — confirmed via grep, no request guard anywhere in this codebase actually blocks a user who ignores `mustChangePassword: true` from calling other endpoints with their still-temporary password, or blocks 2FA-less access before enrollment completes. This is a real, pre-existing gap (it predates this pass — Module 1's own guard pipeline, `JwtAuthGuard → LicenseStateGuard → PermissionsGuard → AuthorityGuard`, never included a "must change password"/"must complete 2FA" gate). "Forced" in this tool's own name means the bootstrap CLI walks the operator through completing both steps as part of provisioning the account — not a new always-on runtime guard. Building that guard is explicitly OUT of scope for this task; flagged here plainly for whoever picks it up.

- `pnpm-workspace.yaml` — added `"tools"` to the `packages` list.
- `packages/server/src/index.ts` — barrel gained `UsersService`/`RolesService`/`AuthService`/`PasswordService`/`TwoFactorService` exports (+ their `CreateUserInput`/`CreateUserResult`/`LoginOutcome`/`PublicUser`/`EnrollResult`/`ActivateResult` types), with a new doc-comment paragraph explaining the narrow, deliberate exception.
- `packages/server/src/platform/users/infrastructure/usr-user.repository.ts` — added `findByUsername()`.
- `packages/server/src/platform/users/application/users.service.ts` — added `findByUsername()` (wraps the above).
- `packages/server/src/platform/users/application/roles.service.ts` — added `listUserIdsForRole()` (wraps `UsrUserRoleRepository.findUserIdsForRole()`, already injected).
- Live dev DB: deleted the superseded `devadmin` fixture (`usr_user`, `usr_user_role`, `usr_session`, `usr_login_event` rows); created the real `bootstrap.admin` System Admin account (`INVITED` → activated on first login, password changed, 2FA enrolled — see verification results above).

## apps/worker — Composition Root + Outbox Dispatcher — 2026-07-28

**Verdict: `apps/worker` now exists, boots as a real second runtime process over the same `@klickit/server` module graph `apps/api` composes (ADR-003's other half), and a real, generic `OutboxDispatcherService` now polls `obx_outbox` on a real BullMQ repeatable job — verified live against the running `klickiterp-postgres-1`/`klickiterp-redis-1` containers: it picked up real inserted rows, stamped `published_at`, correctly no-op'd for an event type with zero registered handlers, and did not reprocess already-published rows on subsequent polls.** This closes the gap the "apps/api Composition Root" section above flagged as still-not-built ("`apps/worker` (the BullMQ/cron runtime — `main.worker.ts`, ADR-003's other half)") and the gap `OutboxWriterService`'s own doc comment named since Module 1 ("Dispatching ... is a future worker concern, deliberately not built here").

### The outbox dispatcher — the one substantive new piece

`packages/server/src/shared/events/outbox-dispatcher.service.ts` (`OutboxDispatcherService`), alongside its siblings under `shared/events/`, per this task's own instruction (testable with this codebase's existing unit-test conventions, then invoked from `apps/worker`). Mechanics:

- `pollOnce(batchSize = 100)` reads `obx_outbox` rows where `published_at IS NULL`, oldest `seq` first (`seq` is the DB `GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY` column — ordering by it is commit order, which is also aggregate order for any single aggregate since all of one aggregate's writes come from the same serialized business transactions), `LIMIT batchSize`.
- For each row, filters the registered handler set (`OUTBOX_HANDLERS` DI token, an array of `{ eventType, consumerName, handle(payload, event) }`, injected `@Optional()` — see below) down to those whose `eventType` matches the row.
- For each matching handler: checks `obx_consumer_mark` for `(consumer=handler.consumerName, event_id=row.id)` FIRST — if present, skips re-invoking (idempotency ledger `OutboxConsumerMarkEntity` was explicitly built for this). Otherwise runs `handler.handle(payload, event)`, then inserts the consumer-mark row. A throw from either step flags the row failed and is logged (`Logger.warn`), never re-invoking a handler whose mark already exists on a later poll — "handler failures retry independently" (docs/phase-3/02-communication-authentication.md §1.3), verbatim.
- Once every matching handler has either succeeded (mark exists) or the row is flagged failed: the row is stamped `published_at = now()` iff nothing failed. A row with **zero** matching handlers is still a valid row to publish immediately (the honest current state — see below). On failure: `attempts` is incremented and left unpublished for the next poll; past 5 attempts a `Logger.error` fires loudly (no dead-letter table, no infinite-retry safeguard beyond this — explicitly out of scope per the task brief).
- Self-healing across a crash: if the process dies after a handler's mark row committed but before `published_at` was stamped, the next poll sees the mark already present, skips re-running that handler, and (once every other matching handler's mark also exists) stamps `published_at` — no handler is ever invoked twice for a row it already completed.
- Overlap guard: an in-instance `isPolling` flag no-ops a `pollOnce()` call that fires while a previous one is still running, on top of `apps/worker`'s own BullMQ `concurrency: 1` (belt-and-suspenders — either alone prevents double-processing).

`packages/server/src/shared/events/outbox-handler.interface.ts` — the `OutboxHandler` interface + `OUTBOX_HANDLERS` Symbol token. `packages/server/src/shared/events/outbox-dispatcher.module.ts` — registers `OutboxDispatcherService` for DI (no `TypeOrmModule.forFeature` needed — reads/writes both entities via `@InjectDataSource()` + `dataSource.getRepository(...)`, same precedent `apps/api/src/health.controller.ts` already established).

**No real business-event handlers were registered — deliberately.** `OUTBOX_HANDLERS` starts unbound; `OutboxDispatcherService`'s `@Optional()` injection defaults to `[]`. Confirmed via grep: no `@OnEvent`/handler classes exist anywhere in this codebase. `platform/comms/application/notifications.service.ts` was explicitly NOT touched — its synchronous adapter-call flow was not converted to a real BullMQ queue, even though both its own doc comment and docs/phase-3/02-communication-authentication.md §1.2's queue topology table name `comms.sms`/`comms.email`/`comms.push` as target BullMQ queues. **This remains unimplemented and is the natural next step for whoever picks up real async job workloads** — converting an already-complete, fully-tested module (1379/1379 tests passing before this pass) carries real regression risk and was not what this task asked for.

### `apps/worker` composition root

Mirrors `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` closely: same `TypeOrmModule.forRoot()` reusing `AppDataSource.options.entities`/`.namingStrategy` (never retyped), same APP-role (`kfe_app`) `DB_USER`/`DB_PASSWORD` credentials, never `DB_MIGRATION_USER`, same `SharedInfraModule` import, same all-22-module import list from `@klickit/server`'s barrel (every module's services need to be DI-available in this process too, since a future real BullMQ processor "living beside its module's services" per ADR-003 would need that). Two differences: `OutboxQueueModule` (this app's own, wires real BullMQ) replaces the Swagger/CORS/prefix concerns `apps/api` owns instead; `HealthController` is `apps/api/src/health.controller.ts` itself, imported by relative path (not forked) — the same established precedent `tools/bootstrap-admin.ts` already set for importing `apps/api`'s `AppModule` this same way, made to type-check via `apps/worker/tsconfig.json` setting `rootDir` to the monorepo root (mirroring `tools/tsconfig.json`'s identical trick).

**Real bootstrap-shape gap found and fixed during this pass**: `NestFactory.create(AppModule)` + `app.listen()` — the same pattern `main.api.ts` uses — does NOT stop at exposing only `HealthController`'s two routes. Nest's HTTP adapter registers EVERY `@Controller()` found anywhere in the imported module tree the instant `app.listen()` is called, so the first live boot attempt put the full ~509-route REST surface live on `WORKER_PORT` too (confirmed empirically: the boot log showed every module's `RouterExplorer` "Mapped {...} route" line — exactly the outcome the task's "no HTTP listener for the main app surface" requirement forbids). Fixed by switching to `NestFactory.createApplicationContext(AppModule)` (full DI graph instantiated, all `OnModuleInit` lifecycle hooks run — including `OutboxQueueModule`'s BullMQ scheduler — but zero HTTP adapter/routes registered) plus a plain `http.createServer()` (`apps/worker/src/main.worker.ts`) serving ONLY `/health`+`/health/ready`, resolving `HealthController` directly from that same application context via `appContext.get()` (reusing the exact same `DataSource`/Redis client instances already open in the process — no duplicate connections — and byte-for-byte identical response shapes, since it's the literal same class/methods, not a re-implementation).

### Real BullMQ wiring

`apps/worker/src/outbox/outbox-queue.module.ts` — `BullModule.forRootAsync()` opens one dedicated `ioredis` connection with `maxRetriesPerRequest: null` (BullMQ's blocking commands require this; deliberately separate from `SharedInfraModule`'s `REDIS_CLIENT`, which uses `maxRetriesPerRequest: 3` for different, non-blocking use cases) plus `BullModule.registerQueue({ name: "outbox-poll" })`. `OutboxPollScheduler` (`OnModuleInit`) registers one repeatable job (`repeat: { every: config.outboxPollIntervalMs }`, default 250ms matching docs/phase-3/02-communication-authentication.md §1.3's own documented cadence; fixed `jobId` so re-registering on every restart doesn't accumulate duplicate schedules). `OutboxPollProcessor` (`@Processor("outbox-poll", { concurrency: 1 })`, extends `WorkerHost`) consumes it and calls `OutboxDispatcherService.pollOnce(config.outboxBatchSize)`. New dependencies: `@nestjs/bullmq@^11.0.4`, `bullmq@^5.81.2` (not previously present anywhere in this monorepo — confirmed via grep before adding); `msgpackr-extract`'s install script needed approving (`pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `onlyBuiltDependencies`, same pattern the existing 3 entries already followed).

Real business queues (`billing`, `comms.*`, `mpesa`, `reports`, `accounting`, `sync`, `webhooks`, `ops` — docs/phase-3/02-communication-authentication.md §1.2's full topology table) remain unimplemented — `outbox-poll` is the only queue this pass registers, carrying only the dispatcher's own poll trigger, exactly as scoped.

### New `AppConfigService` getters / `.env.example`

`workerPort` (default `3001` — `.env.example`'s `WORKER_PORT`, no prior convention existed for this), `outboxPollIntervalMs` (default `250`), `outboxBatchSize` (default `100`) — all follow the existing getter-with-fallback-default pattern verbatim.

### Live verification results — real commands, real output

Booted via `pnpm --filter worker run start:dev` (background process, stdout/stderr redirected to files) against the live containers and the real root `.env`.

1. **Startup log** ended with:
   ```
   [Nest] 13036 - ... LOG [OutboxPollScheduler] Outbox poll repeatable job scheduled — every 250ms
   [Nest] 13036 - ... LOG [Bootstrap] Klickit Finance ERP Worker listening on port 3001 (env: development) — /health, /health/ready only, no REST surface
   [Nest] 13036 - ... LOG [OutboxPollProcessor] Outbox poll: scanned 2, published 2, failed 0
   ```
   No `RouterExplorer`/`Mapped {...} route` lines anywhere in the log — confirmed zero routes registered, unlike the first (fixed) attempt.
2. **`GET http://localhost:3001/health`** → `200`, `{"status":"ok","timestamp":"2026-07-28T10:44:49.966Z"}`.
3. **`GET http://localhost:3001/health/ready`** → `200`, `{"status":"ok","checks":{"database":{"status":"ok"},"redis":{"status":"ok"},"minio":{"status":"skipped",...}}}` — real `SELECT 1` + real Redis `PING`, same shape as `apps/api`'s own.
4. **No REST surface leaked**: `GET http://localhost:3001/users` → `404`; `GET http://localhost:3001/api/v1/auth/login` → `404`.
5. **Real outbox pickup**: inserted 3 real rows into `app.obx_outbox` via `docker exec -i psql` (piped via a `.sql` file/stdin, per the shell-escaping lesson the "apps/api Composition Root" section already documented — never inlined through nested PowerShell/`docker exec`/`psql` quoting), `event_type = 'worker-verification.no-handlers'` (an event type with zero registered handlers, by design). Confirmed unpublished immediately after insert (`published_at` null for all 3), then within one poll cycle the worker's own log showed `Outbox poll: scanned 3, published 3, failed 0`, and a re-query showed all 3 rows' `published_at` stamped — proving the zero-handler case correctly publishes rather than stalling.
6. **No reprocessing**: `SELECT count(*) FILTER (WHERE published_at IS NULL) FROM app.obx_outbox` → `0` after the pickup; subsequent poll ticks logged nothing further (this processor only logs when `scanned > 0`), and no new `Outbox poll:` lines appeared in the following several seconds of continued polling — confirmed the dispatcher does not re-touch already-published rows.
7. **`obx_consumer_mark` stayed empty** (`SELECT count(*)` → `0`) throughout — consistent with zero registered handlers: no handler ever ran, so no mark rows were ever written, exactly the honest state this pass leaves the mechanism in.
8. **Process shutdown verified clean**: killed the full process tree (`ts-node` child + `pnpm` wrapper) via `Stop-Process`, confirmed via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name='node.exe'"` (empty) and `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 3001` (empty) that nothing remained, and a follow-up `GET /health` timed out as expected (connection refused) — same standing verification pattern the "apps/api Composition Root" section established.

`pnpm --filter server exec tsc --noEmit` clean. `pnpm --filter server exec eslint src` → 0 errors, same 5 pre-existing unrelated `no-unused-vars` warnings already documented (no new ones). `apps/worker`'s own `tsc -p tsconfig.json --noEmit` clean (including the cross-package `health.controller.ts` import). `pnpm --filter server test -- --maxWorkers=2` → **175/175 suites, 1390/1390 tests** (the pre-existing 1379-test baseline + 11 new `OutboxDispatcherService` unit tests covering: zero-handler publish, handler invocation + mark recording, eventType filtering, idempotent mark-skip, failure → attempts increment + unpublished, partial-failure batch tolerance, crash-recovery self-healing across two polls, loud-logging threshold, batch-size pass-through, and the overlap guard) — zero regressions. One transient failure was observed on an earlier full-suite run (`banking-e2e.integration.spec.ts`, a live-DB integration test unrelated to any file this pass touched) that passed cleanly both standalone and on a full re-run — confirmed as concurrent-DB-load flakiness from another agent's simultaneous test activity, not a real regression.

## Wallet module design notes for future readers

(1) **Writer-guard-trigger judgement call (BR-WALL-01)**: `wall_wallet` deliberately does NOT get a `trg_gl_writer_guard`-style `application_name` gate the way `gl_journal`/`gl_journal_line`/`gl_period_account_total` do. That GL trigger exists to solve a genuine multi-module fan-in problem (billing, payments, wallet, and every future module all post into the shared GL tables). `wall_wallet.balance` has exactly ONE writer in the entire codebase (`WalletTransactionsService`, via `WallWalletRepository.findByIdForUpdate()`'s row-lock discipline) and no other module registers a repository provider for it — copying the GL pattern here would be solving a fan-in problem this table doesn't have. Defense-in-depth instead comes from `ck_wall_wallet_balance_floor` (DB-layer BR-WALL-01 floor) + `trg_wall_wallet_closed_requires_zero` (BR-WALL-07) + the row-lock discipline, all documented in migration `0090`'s own doc comment.

(2) **`WalletTransactionsService` public API surface** (every method takes the caller's own `EntityManager`, opens no transaction of its own, mirrors `PostingService`/`NumberingService`): `topUp(em, {walletId, amount, method, receiptId?, idempotencyKey?}, actorId)` (P-13), `spend(em, {walletId, amount, servicePointId, items?, idempotencyKey?}, actorId)` (P-14, full limit-check gauntlet: status→daily→per-txn→category-block→balance-floor, each independently rejecting), `transferToFees(em, {walletId, amount, invoiceId, approvalRef?, idempotencyKey?}, actorId)` (P-15, threshold-gated via Settings `wallet.transfer_approval_threshold`, default KES 5000), `transferToWallet(em, {fromWalletId, toWalletId, amount, approvalRef?, idempotencyKey?}, actorId) → {outTransaction, inTransaction}` (P-17, same threshold gate), `refund(em, {walletId, amount, payoutMethod, payoutTarget, approvalRef}, actorId)` (P-16, `approvalRef` always required — no threshold, every refund needs one; BR-WALL-06 payout-target verification against `std_guardian.payout_verified`), `adjust(em, {walletId, amount, direction, reasonCode, idempotencyKey?}, actorId, approvalRef)` (BR-WALL-05, always pre-approved), `closeWallet(em, {walletId, disposition, ...}, actorId)` (BR-WALL-07, idempotent no-op if already CLOSED, rejects a negative/overdraft balance since no disposition can inject funds), `reconcile()` (BR-WALL-08, reuses `accounting`'s `gl_integrity_run` table with `kind='WALLET_RECONCILE'` rather than minting a dedicated table), `lastReconciliation()`.

(3) **Lock-ordering for `transferToWallet()` (P-17)**: both wallets are locked via `WallWalletRepository.findByIdForUpdate()` in ascending-`id`-STRING order (`[fromWalletId, toWalletId].sort()`), never call-argument order — this is what actually prevents a deadlock between two concurrent transfers moving money in opposite directions between the same wallet pair. Unit-tested explicitly (`wallet-transactions.service.spec.ts`'s "locks both wallets in ascending-id order regardless of call-argument order").

(4) **BR-WALL-06's `payout_verified` shape**: `std_guardian.payout_verified` is an opaque `jsonb` column with no shape documented anywhere in the schema docs (carried unread since Module 8). This pass's own interpretation (documented on `RefundPayoutTarget`'s doc comment): a map keyed by payout method, `payoutVerified[method]` truthy = verified for that method. Revisit if a future pass reveals the schema author's actual intended shape.

(5) **`applyInvoiceAllocation()` reuse note**: `transferToFees()` needs the exact "apply an amount to a `bill_invoice`'s `paid_amount`/`balance`/`status`, oldest-installment-first" logic `ReceiptsService.captureReceipt()` already has — but that logic is a *private* method on `ReceiptsService`, not exported from `domains/payments`' barrel, so literal reuse wasn't possible without growing payments' public surface for a 15-line helper. A small local equivalent was built instead in `wallet-transactions.service.ts`, kept in lock-step with the original's algorithm — the task brief's own explicitly-allowed fallback ("else document why you built a small local equivalent").

(6) **Stretch goal NOT attempted**: closing `domains/payments`' documented WALLET-split gap (`ReceiptsService.captureReceipt()`'s `validateSplitReferences()` still rejects `WALLET` splits outright) was scoped as optional and skipped this pass in favor of finishing the full mandatory scope (all 4 controllers, migrations, seed, tests) with margin for verification. Closing it in a future pass means: adding a real runtime dependency from `domains/payments` on `domains/wallet` (currently only an entity-level FK exists, see note 7), and wiring `ReceiptsService` to call something shaped like `WalletTransactionsService.transferToFees()`/a new wallet-debit-to-pay-a-fee method from inside `captureReceipt()`'s own transaction.

(7) **Module 10 gap closure**: migration `0091-add-mpesa-wallet-txn-fk.ts` adds the real `fk_pay_mpesa_transaction_wallet_txn` FK (`ON DELETE RESTRICT`, reversible `down()`) that Module 10 left as a bare `uuid` column since `wall_transaction` didn't exist yet. `PayMpesaTransactionEntity` now has a real `@ManyToOne` to `WallTransactionEntity`, imported directly from its entity file (never through `domains/wallet`'s barrel) — this makes wallet↔payments bidirectional at the entity level, the same justified-and-documented shape `module-deps.json`'s students↔billing pair established first. `domains/payments`' own `mayImport` list gained `domains/wallet` (entity-only — no new runtime service call).

(8) **Still open**: `bill_refund_voucher.b2c_transaction_id → pay_mpesa_transaction` (Module 9's original forward-reference gap) remains unclosed as of this note — it was in scope to consider but out of THIS module's own file-touch boundary (would mean editing `domains/billing` for a gap that isn't Wallet's own); still optional/future, same as every prior module's resume-point note has restated.

## Established conventions (don't re-derive, just follow)

- Every module built in ~2 agent passes: (1) foundation — entities extending `BaseEntity`/`MutableBaseEntity`, repositories, migration; (2) application — services (transactions via `tx()` helper), controllers (`@RequirePermission` guarded, Swagger-documented), DTOs, events, tests.
- Permission catalogue is ONE file: `packages/server/src/platform/users/domain/permission-catalogue.ts` — every module appends its codes there, never creates a parallel catalogue.
- `packages/config/eslint/module-deps.json` is the import-boundary manifest — update it whenever a new module is added or a legitimate cross-module dependency is introduced (see the `platform/auth → platform/users` precedent and its documented rationale in that file).
- Migration `0900-seed-permissions-and-roles.ts` is extended (not replaced) by every module that adds permission codes — idempotent upsert pattern, follow what's already there.
- Money uses `shared/money` (`Money` class + `MoneyTransformer`), never floats. IDs use `shared/ids/uuid7.ts`. Secrets/config use `shared/crypto/aes-gcm.util.ts`. Config access via `shared/config/app-config.service.ts`.
- Integration tests self-skip via a `beforeAll` connectivity probe against `packages/server/src/migrations/data-source.ts` when Postgres is unreachable — keep this pattern so `pnpm --filter server test` never fails just because Docker isn't up yet.
- Known toolchain gotchas: ESLint 9 needs a per-package `eslint.config.js` entrypoint (not just the shared `packages/config/eslint/index.js`); `otplib` must stay on v12 (v13 breaks ts-jest); `pnpm-workspace.yaml` needs real `onlyBuiltDependencies`.
- No `any` where avoidable, strict TypeScript, no WHAT-comments — spec-ID comments (`FR-SET-003.1` etc.) at the exact enforcing line are fine and encouraged. Controllers stay thin; services own transactions.
- Every agent pass must end with `pnpm --filter server exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm --filter server exec eslint src`, and `pnpm --filter server test` all green before reporting done.

Previously, 2026-07-21, Module 19 (Integrations) completed in a SINGLE comprehensive pass — Module 19 is now ✅ **COMPLETE, verified** (full write-up in the Module status table's Module 19 row above). Small physical schema (3 tables: `intg_webhook_subscription`/`intg_webhook_delivery`/`intg_sync_log`) but real mechanical substance, per the task brief's own framing (same scope-shape as Module 5/Module 11's single-pass builds): webhook delivery with HMAC signing/exponential-ish retry/72h auto-disable, plus 3 real OAuth2-token-exchange accounting-sync adapters (QuickBooks/Xero/Sage) behind `AccountingSyncPort`, resolved via `platform/settings`' `IntegrationConfigService` exactly like `platform/comms`' own `AdapterResolverService`. **Signature header** (FR-INTG-007.1, worked example in `webhook-delivery.service.ts`'s own doc comment): `X-Klickit-Signature: t=<unix-seconds>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256 of "{timestamp}.{rawJsonBody}", keyed by the subscription's decrypted secret>`. **8-step backoff schedule** (`WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_SCHEDULE_MINUTES`, delay after the Nth failure): 1min/5min/15min/1h/3h/6h/12h/24h — cumulative time from first failure to the START of the 8th (final) attempt ≈ 22h21m (sum of the first 7 deltas), matching "retries 8× over 24 hours"; attempt 8's own failure goes straight to `DEAD` rather than a 9th retry. **Auto-disable** tracked on the SUBSCRIPTION via `failure_streak_started_at` (set on first failure since last success, cleared on success, checked on every subsequent failure against a 72h threshold) — flips `is_active=false`+`disabled_reason` and sends a best-effort `platform/comms` admin EMAIL alert once tripped; a real bug was caught during testing and fixed (`registerFailureAndMaybeDisable()` originally used `new Date()` instead of `Date.now()`, which doesn't respect a `Date.now` jest spy and made the 72h path non-deterministic/untestable — fixed for consistency with the rest of the service's `Date.now()`-based timestamp math). `WebhookHttpClient` (the real signed-POST transport) was deliberately extracted into its own injectable class rather than inlined, specifically so `attemptDelivery()`'s status-transition logic could be unit-tested against a mocked client with zero real network/mocking gymnastics. `module-deps.json`'s new `domains/integrations` entry is DELIBERATELY NARROW (`mayImport: ["shared", "platform/settings", "platform/users", "platform/comms"]`, NO `domains/*` sibling at all) — the opposite design point from Module 18/Reporting's deliberately-BROAD entry: this module transmits what other modules hand it rather than independently querying other domains, so `intg_sync_log.entity`/`.entity_id` stay a deliberately loose no-FK reference (mirrors `appr_instance`'s own precedent). 6 new permission codes appended (`integrations:webhook:view/manage/retry`, `integrations:sync:push/test/view`); `0900` seed needed ZERO code changes (fully generic `PERMISSION_CATALOGUE` loop, same confirmed-no-op precedent Module 18 established) and no workflow/GL-account seeds (no approval chains, no GL posting). 5 new spec files (27 new tests: exact hand-computed HMAC signature assertion, full 8-attempt backoff/DEAD-transition walk, 72h auto-disable incl. the under-threshold negative case, `AccountingSyncService.pushEntity()`'s log-then-classify behavior for both outcomes, `AccountingSyncResolverService`'s fallback/cache/re-resolve mirroring `platform/comms`' own resolver spec) plus `integrations-e2e.integration.spec.ts` (self-skips without Docker — create subscription → `dispatch()` queues a real delivery row → `AccountingSyncService.pushEntity()` round-trips a real `intg_sync_log` row). 1280/1280 total passing (1253 baseline + 27 new, no regressions), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint src` clean, full suite ran clean with `--maxWorkers=2`. **Next module is 20 (Backups/Ops).**

2026-07-20, Module 18 (Reporting Engine + Dashboard) **PASS A** completed on top of the foundation pass below — 🔶 **PASS A DONE (report registry + 7 report-of-record financial/ledger reports), Pass B pending (remaining domain reports, Dashboard KPI service reading the MVs, export/CSV generation, scheduling, controllers, permission catalogue, seed)**. Built `packages/server/src/domains/reporting/application/`: `report-registry.service.ts` — `ReportDefinition` interface (`{code, name, domain, permissionCode, paramsShape, columns, execute(params, context)}`, `ReportResult = {rows, totals?, generatedAt}` per FR-RPT-001.1) plus `ReportRegistryService.register()`/`.get()`/`.list()`, a small dependency-free class with no constructor params (trivially unit-testable with synthetic `ReportDefinition` objects). **Registry wiring pattern (documented per the task brief's own "your call" instruction)**: each of the 7 report classes below is an ordinary `@Injectable()` provider implementing `ReportDefinition` directly (no self-registration from its own constructor); `reporting.module.ts` implements `OnModuleInit` and calls `registry.register()` once per report AFTER Nest's DI container has fully constructed every provider — the "static array assembled in reporting.module.ts" option, chosen over self-registration for independent testability. Each `register()` call is written out individually (not looped over a mixed-type array) because `register<TParams>()` is generic per report and a loop over the whole array would force TypeScript to infer one shared `TParams` across mutually-incompatible `execute()` signatures. `saved-params.service.ts` — CRUD `rpt_saved_params`, every read/update/delete scoped to the caller's own `userId`; a saved-params row owned by a different user is reported as `NotFoundException`, identically to a row that doesn't exist, so a caller can never probe another user's saved report names by id. **7 report-of-record reports, all reading the real ledger directly, never the 5 materialized views (FR-RPT-008 — "report-of-record queries bypass MVs and read the ledger directly")**: `trial-balance.report.ts` (params `{periodId}`; every postable `gl_account` summed from `gl_period_account_total` for THAT period only across cost centers; the debit=credit check is a visible computed `totals.balanced` field, never a thrown error — a genuine imbalance is a real finding to surface, not hide). `balance-sheet.report.ts` (params `{periodId}`, ASSET/LIABILITY/EQUITY only; **CUMULATIVE**: sums `gl_period_account_total` across every period with the same `fiscal_year_id` and `seq <= target.seq` — "up to and including" the given period, since a balance-sheet account's balance is to-date, not one period's movement). `income-statement.report.ts` (params `{fromPeriodId, toPeriodId}`, INCOME/EXPENSE only; **PERIOD-RANGE ONLY, NOT cumulative** — sums only periods with `fromPeriod.seq <= seq <= toPeriod.seq`, since P&L accounts reset every fiscal year; rejects a range spanning two different `fiscal_year_id`s with `ValidationException` — this is the key designed contrast with `BalanceSheetReport`'s cumulative-from-year-start behavior). `general-ledger.report.ts` (params `{accountId, fromDate, toDate}`; a raw `DataSource.query()` join of `gl_journal_line`/`gl_journal` — the same cross-table raw-SQL style `IntegritySweepService.runSweep()` established — with a pre-range opening-balance query seeding a cumulative running-balance walk). `cashbook.report.ts` (params `{fromDate, toDate}`; same detail-join shape as General Ledger, pre-filtered to a **hardcoded** `DEFAULT_CASHBOOK_ACCOUNT_CODES = ["1010","1020","1030","1040"]` — Petty Cash/Bank-Operating/Cheques-in-Transit/Card-POS-Clearing, matching `0900-seed-permissions-and-roles.ts`'s actual seeded `COA_TEMPLATE` codes — because `gl_account.control_domain`'s CHECK enum has no `CASH`/`BANK` value at all, the same category of judgement call as this codebase's clearing-account resolvers; merges all matched accounts into one chronological listing with a combined running balance plus a `totals.byAccount` per-account breakdown). `student-statement.report.ts` (params `{studentId}`; a thin wrapper delegating directly to `domains/students`' `StudentLedgerService.getStatement()`, which already returns a report-of-record running-balance statement — no ledger logic duplicated here). `aging-outstanding.report.ts` (params `{asOfDate?}`; queries `bill_invoice` via a NEW `BillInvoiceRepository.findAllOpen()` method added to `domains/billing`'s infrastructure this pass — every `balance > 0 AND status <> 'VOID'` invoice across all students, unlike the existing `findOverdueOpen()` which excludes not-yet-due rows; grouped per student into 4 pivoted bucket columns (`0-30`/`31-60`/`61-90`/`90+`, the same boundary convention `mv_ar_summary`'s own migration SQL documents, not-yet-due invoices folding into `0-30`), with per-student `total` rows plus grand `totals` across all students). **`module-deps.json`'s `domains/reporting` entry extended** to `["shared", "accounting", "platform/settings", "platform/users", "platform/files", "domains/students", "domains/billing"]` — two new REAL runtime service/repository dependencies (not just entity-only): `domains/students` (`StudentLedgerService.getStatement()`, `StdStudentRepository.findById()`) and `domains/billing` (`BillInvoiceRepository.findAllOpen()`), both via their index.ts barrels only. `reporting.module.ts` now imports `AccountingModule`/`StudentsModule`/`BillingModule` (not just entities). 9 new spec files, 33 new tests, 1210/1210 total passing (1177 baseline + 33 new, no regressions), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint src` clean — the full suite ran clean on the first attempt at default worker concurrency in ~51s; the foundation pass's jest-worker OOM workaround (`--maxWorkers=2`) was **NOT needed** this time. Confirmed still NO `api/`/`events/` folder, controllers, permission-catalogue codes, or `0900` seed changes were built in this pass. **Next action is Module 18's own Pass B** — remaining domain reports (payroll, expenses, suppliers, wallet, fee collection, receipts/invoices, tax/audit), the Dashboard KPI service (reads the MVs), export/CSV generation, scheduling execution, controllers, permission catalogue, seed.
