Previously, 2026-07-20, Module 17 (Fixed Assets) **application-layer pass** completed on top of the foundation pass below — Module 17 is now ✅ **COMPLETE, verified**, and is **the LAST of the "operations cluster" domain modules (12 Procurement through 17 Fixed Assets — all 6 now complete)**. `domains/fixed-assets/application/` — `CategoriesService` (CRUD `fa_category`, requires all 3 GL account mappings to actually exist at creation, `residual_pct` validated in `[0,1]`, `rate` required>0 when `method='RB'` — none of this DB-enforced, all service-layer defense per the foundation pass's own doc comments). `AssetsService` (CRUD `fa_asset`; `create()` derives `residual_value` from `category.residual_pct × cost` when the caller omits it; `search()`/`findByBarcode()` delegate straight to the foundation pass's `FaAssetRepository` finders; `updateCondition()` takes an optional trailing `EntityManager` so `VerificationService.post()` can compose it inside its own transaction). **`DepreciationRunsService`, the core engine**: `createRun()` populates one `fa_depreciation_line` per `FaAssetRepository.findActiveForDepreciation()` result — SL charges `(cost-residual_value)/life_months` (via a new local `divideMoneyByInt()` util, `Money` has no public divide-by-int op, same pattern `payroll/loans.service.ts`'s own private helper already established); RB charges `nbv×rate/12`, `nbv` RE-DERIVED every run from the asset's CURRENT `accum_depreciation` (the defining reducing-balance trait — each period's charge shrinks). **Day-count proration** (this pass's own documented convention, the task left the exact scheme open): ONLY the period whose `[starts_on,ends_on]` contains `asset.in_service_from` is prorated — ratio = (days from `in_service_from` through the period's last day inclusive) / (total days in the period); every later period gets the full charge, no further proration. **BR-FA-01 cap/skip** is real enforcement here, not just the DB CHECK backstop: `headroom = (cost-residual_value)-accum_depreciation` computed before charging, `charge = min(computed, headroom)`, and a capped-to-≤0 charge means the asset is skipped entirely (no zero-amount line ever inserted). `post()` requires `PENDING_APPROVAL` + a real `APPROVED` `DEPRECIATION` `appr_instance` (verified via `ApprovalEngineService.getStatus()` directly — `fa_depreciation_run`'s 3-value enum has no `APPROVED` state of its own, same "verify the real appr_instance column" pattern `StockTakesService` established) — lines are grouped by the asset's `category_id` (each category owns its OWN `gl_dep_expense_account_id`/`gl_accum_dep_account_id` pair) and exactly ONE debit/credit pair emitted per category, NOT per asset, in ONE `PostingService.post()` call; every affected asset's `accum_depreciation` incremented afterward. **`DisposalService`** mirrors `bank_transfer`'s real 4-value `DRAFT→PENDING_APPROVAL→APPROVED→POSTED` shape (a genuine `APPROVED` state exists, unlike the depreciation run) — `create()` computes `gain_loss = proceeds - (cost-accum_depreciation)` and catches `uq_fa_disposal_asset_id`'s unique-violation into `ConflictException`; `submitForApproval()`'s `amount` is `proceeds` when positive else `|gain_loss|` (documented judgement call — a WRITE_OFF/DONATION with zero proceeds should still show the approver its real book-value impact); `post()` realizes P-31 with a new `gl-disposal-accounts.util.ts` (`DISPOSAL_PROCEEDS_ACCOUNT_CODE="1020"` reusing the already-seeded Bank-Operating leaf — `domains/payments`/`domains/banking` are NOT in this module's `mayImport` list, so the task brief's own "simple documented default" escape hatch was taken instead of widening the dependency surface; `GAIN_ON_DISPOSAL_ACCOUNT_CODE="4050"`/`LOSS_ON_DISPOSAL_ACCOUNT_CODE="5110"` new CoA leaves) — debits proceeds (skipped when zero, never posts a zero-amount line) + accum_depreciation (skipped when zero) + `|gain_loss|` on whichever side it belongs, credits the category's cost account for the FULL cost; this ALWAYS balances by construction since `gain_loss` is algebraically defined as `proceeds+accum_depreciation-cost`. **`VerificationService`** mirrors `StockTakesService`'s exact shape per the task's own instruction (`fa_verification.status` has the identical 6-value enum, no `APPROVED` value either, same `getStatus()`-verification pattern) — `scope: {assetIds: string[]|"ALL"}` (`"ALL"` resolves to every currently `ACTIVE` asset, no `fa_store`-equivalent table exists to scope by). **Completeness-signal design note**: `fa_verification_line.found` is NOT NULL and its `false` default deliberately doubles as both "unexamined" and "confirmed missing" (per the foundation pass's own doc comment), so unlike `inv_stock_take_line.countedQty`'s nullable signal, `recordCounts()` repurposes `notes` instead — an omitted `notes` defaults to `""` (never left `null`) the first time a line is touched, so "every line recorded" is checked via `notes !== null`. `post()` compiles every `found=false` line's `assetId` into a plain `missingAssetIds` report (NOT a schema table, NOT automatic disposal) — a human acts on each entry separately via `DisposalService.create({method:'WRITE_OFF',...})`; found lines with a recorded `condition` get it applied via `AssetsService.updateCondition()`, composed in the same transaction. `TransfersService`/`MaintenanceService` are simpler: `TransfersService.create()` captures `from_location`/`from_custodian` from the asset's CURRENT values before overwriting them, `acknowledge()` is a lightweight no-approval receipt confirmation; `MaintenanceService.schedule()` flips `fa_asset.status='UNDER_MAINTENANCE'` immediately (this pass's own judgement call on timing), `complete()` flips it back to `ACTIVE` and optionally links an ALREADY-CREATED `exp_voucher` id (never creates one itself, one-directional coupling only). `api/` — 7 controllers (`categories`/`assets`/`depreciation-runs`/`transfers`/`maintenance`/`disposal`/`verification`), full DTOs (Money as decimal strings)/Swagger, `dto/decimal.util.ts`; 15 new permission codes appended to `platform/users/domain/permission-catalogue.ts`. `fixed-assets.module.ts` now imports `AccountingModule`/`SettingsModule`/`ApprovalsModule` as real Nest modules (not `ProcurementModule`/`ExpensesModule` — those cross-module FKs stay compile-time-only entity targets, no sibling service ever called at runtime) — **all 7 controllers explicitly verified present in the `controllers` array** (per this task's own standing warning about a past near-miss). `0900-seed-permissions-and-roles.ts` extended: `DEPRECIATION`/`ASSET_DISPOSALS`/`ASSET_VERIFICATION` (a NEW domain code this pass introduces — `STOCK_ADJUSTMENTS` didn't conceptually fit a physical asset count) added to `SINGLE_LEVEL_WORKFLOW_SEEDS`; `COA_TEMPLATE` extended with `4050 Gain on Disposal`/`5110 Loss on Disposal` plus 6 dedicated leaves (`1800`/`1805`/`5120` and `1820`/`1825`/`5130`) for two starter `fa_category` rows ("Furniture & Fittings" SL/60mo, "IT Equipment" SL/36mo) seeded via a new `seedFixedAssetCategories()`. 61 new tests (7 unit spec files — `depreciation-runs.service.spec.ts` covers hand-computed SL/RB math, a hand-computed 16/31-day proration example, and the BR-FA-01 cap/skip cases explicitly, plus the P-30 per-category-aggregation assertions; `disposal.service.spec.ts` covers gain/loss/zero-proceeds-skip-the-cash-line P-31 assertions explicitly; `verification.service.spec.ts` covers the missing-asset write-off-proposal compilation explicitly — plus `categories`/`assets`/`transfers`/`maintenance` specs — and the `fixed-assets-e2e.integration.spec.ts` capstone: register asset → depreciation run create→submit→REAL-approve→post [P-30] → dispose create→submit→decide→post [P-31], asserting balanced GL at both steps and the asset's final `status='DISPOSED'`/correct `accum_depreciation`, self-skips without Docker) — 1173/1173 total passing (1112 baseline + 61 new, no regressions), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint src` clean. **Next module is 18 (Reporting Engine + Dashboard)** — the first of the platform-wrap-up modules, and a genuinely DIFFERENT architectural shape from every module built so far: it needs to READ ACROSS every prior module's data (billing/payments/wallet/procurement/inventory/expenses/payroll/banking/fixed-assets, 16 modules' worth of tables) rather than owning one clean vertical slice with its own writes — worth planning carefully before diving in (query/aggregation strategy, whether it needs its own read-replica-style repository layer or reuses each module's existing repositories directly, dashboard refresh/caching strategy). **BR-BANK-03 cross-module gap (flagged in Module 16's own row) remains open and unaddressed by this pass**: a period's bank reconciliation must be LOCKED before that period can be HARD_CLOSED, but `accounting`'s `FiscalYearsService.hardClosePeriod()` still doesn't check `bank_reconciliation` — still a real follow-up item for whichever future pass touches period-close logic.

Previously, 2026-07-20, Module 17 (Fixed Assets) **foundation pass** completed — 🔶 **FOUNDATION DONE, application layer pending** (this module is NOT finished; next action is Module 17's own application-layer pass, NOT Module 18). `domains/fixed-assets/domain/` — 9 entities per docs/phase-4/04-schema-operations.md §5 (bottom half): `fa_category`, `fa_asset`, `fa_depreciation_run`, `fa_depreciation_line`, `fa_maintenance`, `fa_transfer`, `fa_disposal`, `fa_verification`, `fa_verification_line` (the DDL's own "+ fa_depreciation_line"/"+ line child" inline shorthands realized as two real physical child tables, the same treatment `inv_stock_take_line`/`proc_grn_line` established for identical shorthand). `FaCategoryEntity` (config, genuine post-creation policy/GL-remapping edits) and `FaAssetEntity`/`FaDepreciationRunEntity`/`FaMaintenanceEntity`/`FaTransferEntity`/`FaDisposalEntity`/`FaVerificationEntity` (status-progression workflow documents, `FaAssetEntity`'s own justification being its genuine ongoing `accum_depreciation`/`status`/`location`/`custodian` progression) all extend `MutableBaseEntity`. **Line-table base-class judgement calls**: `FaDepreciationLineEntity` → plain `BaseEntity` — computed once by the next pass's SL/RB run engine and effectively frozen alongside its parent once the run posts (`trg_fa_depreciation_run_immutable`), the same "computed once, frozen alongside the parent" shape `GlJournalLineEntity` established; `FaVerificationLineEntity` → `MutableBaseEntity` — `found`/`condition`/`notes` are filled in DURING the counting phase, a real post-creation edit window, the exact same reasoning `InvStockTakeLineEntity.countedQty` used. **`fa_disposal.status` enum design call** (the DDL gives this column no spelled-out CHECK value list, unlike every other status column in this schema): `DRAFT|PENDING_APPROVAL|APPROVED|POSTED` — mirrors `bank_transfer`'s 4-value shape (a disposal's approval decision and its actual P-31 GL posting are two genuinely separate moments, needing a dedicated `APPROVED` state between them) rather than `fa_depreciation_run`'s leaner 3-value enum, documented in full on `FaDisposalEntity`'s own doc comment. **`fa_verification`/`fa_verification_line` mirror `inv_stock_take`/`inv_stock_take_line` exactly** per the DDL's own explicit instruction — identical 6-value status enum (`OPEN->COUNTING->REVIEW->PENDING_APPROVAL->POSTED`/`CANCELLED`), `scope` jsonb carrying the arbitrary asset/location/category selector (Fixed Assets has no `fa_store`-equivalent table, so unlike `inv_stock_take.store_id` there is no dedicated location FK); FR-FA-007.1's "missing-asset report -> write-off proposals" (a verification finding an asset missing eventually feeding `fa_disposal` with `method='WRITE_OFF'`) is explicitly left a service-layer concern for the next pass. **Shared BR-FA-02 trigger-function design**: a single `fn_check_asset_not_disposed()` PL/pgSQL function (reads `NEW.asset_id` generically, since `fa_maintenance`/`fa_transfer`/`fa_depreciation_line` all name that FK column identically) raises an exception whenever the referenced `fa_asset.status IN ('DISPOSED','WRITTEN_OFF')`, attached via THREE separate `BEFORE INSERT CREATE TRIGGER` statements (`trg_fa_maintenance_no_txn_after_disposal`/`trg_fa_transfer_no_txn_after_disposal`/`trg_fa_depreciation_line_no_txn_after_disposal`) all calling the same function rather than duplicating the check three times — collectively realizing the task brief's named `trg_fa_asset_no_txn_after_disposal`. **Two unconditional-freeze immutability triggers**: `trg_fa_depreciation_run_immutable` and `trg_fa_disposal_immutable`, both `BEFORE UPDATE`, both rejecting ALL updates once `status='POSTED'` with NO "status may still progress" carve-out — deliberately simpler than `pyrl_run`'s own immutability trigger, since `POSTED` is each entity's own terminal state (their enums end there, unlike `pyrl_run`'s longer `COMMITTED->PAID->FILED` tail). No `trg_gl_writer_guard`-style `application_name`-checking trigger was added — same judgement call every prior module this size has made (single-writer-service discipline deemed sufficient), documented in the migration's own doc comment. Real cross-module FKs, all entity-only (no sibling *service* called at runtime): `GlAccountEntity`/`GlPeriodEntity`/`GlJournalEntity` (`accounting`, barrel imports), `UsrUserEntity` (`platform/users`, barrel import), `ProcSupplierEntity`/`ProcPurchaseOrderEntity`/`ProcGrnEntity` (`domains/procurement`, direct entity-file imports per this codebase's circular-require-avoidance discipline — NOT bidirectional, unlike banking<->procurement, since Procurement never needs to reference `fa_asset` back), `ExpVoucherEntity` (`domains/expenses`, direct entity-file import, also one-directional). `module-deps.json`'s new `domains/fixed-assets` entry (`mayImport: ["shared", "accounting", "platform/settings", "platform/approvals", "platform/users", "domains/procurement", "domains/expenses"]`). `infrastructure/` — 9 plain repositories plus the forward-looking finders: `FaAssetRepository.findActiveForDepreciation()` (ACTIVE assets with NBV still above residual — the next pass's depreciation-run population query) and `.searchByCodeOrBarcode()` (plain `ILIKE`, not trigram — the DDL names no `GIN trgm` index for `fa_asset`, unlike `inv_item`/`proc_supplier`), `FaDepreciationRunRepository.findByPeriodId()` (the UQ's own lookup), `FaDisposalRepository.findByAssetId()` (the UQ's own lookup — one disposal per asset, ever). `fixed-assets.module.ts`/`index.ts` — deliberately minimal (entities + repositories only, no `application/`/`api/`/`events/`), mirroring every prior module's own foundation pass. Migration `0150-create-fixed-assets-tables.ts` — all 9 tables matching the entities exactly (incl. `ck_fa_asset_accum_dep_within_depreciable_base` for BR-FA-01, `uq_fa_disposal_asset_id`, `uq_fa_depreciation_line_run_asset`, the partial unique `fa_asset.barcode` index, the `photos uuid[]` array column) + the 3 triggers described above, real reversible `down()`. `data-source.ts` updated with all 9 new entities. One integration test `fixed-assets-triggers.integration.spec.ts` (9-table reachability + a combined BR-FA-02 test proving the shared trigger function allows inserts into all three dependent tables while the asset is ACTIVE and blocks all three once DISPOSED + both `_immutable` triggers' unconditional-freeze behavior, real INSERT/UPDATE against Postgres, self-skips without Docker) — 12 new tests, 1112/1112 total passing (1100 baseline + 12 new, no regressions), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint src` clean. Confirmed NO `application/`/`api/`/`events/` folders, services, controllers, permission-catalogue codes, or `0900` seed changes were built in this pass. **Next action is Module 17's own application-layer pass** (asset register CRUD, the monthly depreciation-run engine [SL: `(cost-residual)/life_months`; RB: `nbv×rate/12`; prorated from in-service month, batch journal P-30, approval-gated `DEPRECIATION`, schedule regenerated on revaluation/transfer], the disposal wizard [gain/loss computation, approval `ASSET_DISPOSALS`, P-31], transfers, maintenance, physical verification sessions, controllers, tests, seed) — NOT Module 18, since this module isn't finished yet.

See "Communications module design notes" below for the send-orchestrator and broadcast-audience-resolution design decisions.

**Communications module design notes for future readers**: (1) `NotificationsService.send()` is a "write-first" audit log, not a request/response call that can fail — it inserts `comm_message` with `status='QUEUED'` *before* touching the adapter (so a crash mid-send still leaves a row), then updates that same row to `SENT`/`FAILED` in place; adapter exceptions are caught and recorded as `FAILED`+`error`, never rethrown, matching the append-only nature of the table (a failed send is a valid outcome to log, not an exceptional condition the caller must handle). The final `SENT` transition and `MessageSentEvent` outbox write happen together in one transaction so the event can never announce a send that isn't actually persisted as `SENT`; `FAILED`/`OPTED_OUT` outcomes publish no event (nothing to announce). Real async queueing (`comms.sms`/`comms.email`/`comms.push` BullMQ queues, docs/phase-3/02-communication-authentication.md §1.2) is explicitly out of scope — no worker app exists in this repo yet, so `send()` calls the adapter synchronously in the caller's process; wiring this through BullMQ is a future worker-module concern that should be able to reuse `NotificationsService.send()`'s body unchanged inside a job processor. (2) `BroadcastsService.resolveAudience()` is two-step: `resolveUsers()` turns the `AudienceDef` (`STAFF_ROLE`/`EXPLICIT_USER_IDS`, the only two kinds supported — guardian/parent kinds are deferred to Module 8/Students, not built yet) into `UsrUserEntity[]` via `UsersService`'s public surface only, then a channel-specific mapping step turns each user into zero-or-more concrete recipient addresses: `EMAIL`→`user.email`, `SMS`/`WHATSAPP`→`user.phone`, `PUSH`→every one of that user's `comm_device_token` rows (so a user with two devices yields two `comm_message` rows), `INAPP`→the user's own id (no external transport, `comm_message` rows themselves are what a future WebSocket/notification-badge consumer reads). Users missing the channel's needed contact field are silently skipped, which is why `recipientCount` is set from the *resolved* recipient list, not the raw role/id-list size — a role can easily contain staff with no phone number, and that's not an error. (3) `AdapterResolverService` caches one adapter instance per channel keyed by the resolved `set_integration_config` row's id (avoids re-creating an SMTP transporter / Firebase app on every send); the outgoing `FcmPushAdapter` is explicitly `dispose()`d when the enabled FCM config changes, since the Admin SDK keys `App` instances by name in a process-global registry and would otherwise leak one entry per config change. (4) Out of scope, deliberately: real async queueing/retry via BullMQ (see (1)); guardian/parent broadcast audience resolution (Module 8/Students doesn't exist); approval-engine integration for `BroadcastsService.submitForApproval()`/`.approve()` — Module 6 (Approvals) isn't built yet, so `submitForApproval()` only stores the caller-supplied `approvalRef` with no validation, and `.approve()` is a stand-in for the real decision; a domain-event-driven trigger dispatcher that would actually read `comm_trigger_binding` rows and call `NotificationsService.send()` automatically (no such dispatcher exists anywhere in this codebase yet — `TriggerBindingsService` only stores/serves the configuration).

**Branding module design notes for future readers**: (1) `publish()`/`revert()` share the same unset-then-set transactional pattern as `AcademicCalendarService.setCurrentYear`/`.setCurrentTerm` — archive the previous PUBLISHED row inside the same transaction as setting the new one, so `uq_brnd_theme_published_p` is never violated mid-flight; `publish()` on an already-PUBLISHED target is a no-op (returns unchanged, no event). (2) `update()` refuses to edit a theme once `status='PUBLISHED'` — the Draft → Preview → Publish → Revert workflow (FR-BRND-002.1) means editing what's currently live requires creating a new draft, not mutating the row serving the login page/documents right now; this guard is this service's own design choice, not explicitly spelled out in the DDL. (3) `getCurrentTheme()`'s hardcoded fallback and the `0900` seed migration's default theme row both source their palette/tokens from the exact same `INFONEY_DEFAULT_*` constants (`application/infoney-default-theme.ts`) — deliberately, so the boot-time fallback (before any seed has run) and the eventually-seeded row never drift apart. (4) Radius (sm/md/lg/xl, 4/8/16/24px) and spacing (4/8/12/16/24/32/48px) scale *values* were this module's own sensible-default choice — the task brief specified the color palette/font exactly but left the scale numbers open beyond "e.g. radius sm/md/lg/xl" / "spacing scale 4/8/12/16/24/32/48". (5) `@Public()` is imported from `platform/auth`'s barrel (`index.ts`) rather than duplicated — a new one-directional `module-deps.json` exception, same shape as the `platform/auth → platform/users` precedent. (6) Out of scope, deliberately: actual dashboard/PDF rendering that *consumes* `buildThemeCssVariables()`'s output (frontend/reporting-engine concern per the task brief); a version-history/new-draft-from-published convenience endpoint (callers currently `create()` a fresh draft with the desired changes and `publish()` it); cache-busting on `ThemePublishedEvent` (no subscriber exists yet, same as every other module's outbox events so far).

**Files module design notes for future readers**: (1) `upload()` is deliberately not a distributed transaction — storage write happens first, then the DB insert; if the DB insert fails, a best-effort compensating storage delete is attempted and the original error is rethrown regardless of whether the compensating delete itself succeeds (files aren't financial data, unlike `gl_journal`, so this documented inconsistency window is acceptable — see the service's doc comment). (2) `object_key` is `uuid7/sanitized-original-name` — collision-resistant and roughly chronological. (3) Branding (Module 4, now complete) was the first real consumer of this module's public surface (`brnd_theme.logo_file_id`/`favicon_file_id` FK to `file_object`) — imported via `platform/files`'s `index.ts` barrel only, per `crossSiblingImportPolicy`.

**Approvals module design notes for future readers**: (1) `appr_instance` has no `level_subset`/`matched_rule_id` column in the DDL, so routing/level resolution is **re-derived, not persisted** — `ApprovalEngineService`'s private `resolveApplicableLevels()` re-runs the same deterministic matching algorithm at `submit()`, `decide()`, and `listPendingForApprover()` time, keyed off `instance.amount` (frozen at submission per BR-APPR-02) and the initiator's *current* `department_id`. A routing rule matches when `amount` falls in `[min_amount, max_amount)` (min inclusive, max exclusive — this module's own reasonable choice, not stated in the DDL) AND, if the rule sets `department_id`, the initiator's own `department_id` equals it — department is derived from the initiator rather than accepted as a `submit()` input parameter, since the task's own `submit()` signature has no department field. The first matching rule (ordered by `min_amount` ASC) wins; no match falls back to ALL levels. This same matched rule's `department_id` is what `DEPT_HEAD`-type levels check against for their single candidate (the department's `head_user_id`) — a `DEPT_HEAD` level under a routing setup with no department-scoped rule has no resolvable candidate, a documented limitation. (2) Authorization (`resolveAuthorizedActor`) checks direct membership first (ROLE via `UsersService.listActiveUsersByRoleId()`, USERS via `user_ids`, DEPT_HEAD via the department head), then falls through to one-hop delegation (`DelegationsService.resolveEffectiveApprover`) for each candidate — self-approval (BR-APPR-01 "including via delegation or role overlap") is rejected both when `actorId === initiator_id` directly AND when the *legitimate approver whose authority is being exercised* (the delegator, if delegation was used) is the initiator, on top of the DB-layer `trg_appr_no_self_approval` trigger (three-layer defense, G-04). (3) `ApprWorkflowVersionEntity` is a **documented deviation** from the task brief's "MutableBaseEntity for all config entities" default: its own DDL column is itself named `version` (the workflow's sequential version number, part of `uq(workflow_def_id, version)`), which collides with `MutableBaseEntity`'s `@VersionColumn` of the same name — it extends `BaseEntity` instead and has no optimistic-lock column; every mutation already runs inside `tx()` with explicit find-then-save. (4) `InstancesController.cancel()` is deliberately NOT `@RequirePermission`-guarded (self-service for the initiator, mirroring `DeviceTokensController`'s precedent), with a `req.user.roles.includes("System Admin")` check standing in for the task brief's "or an admin permission" clause — a pragmatic simplification since this codebase's JWT claims carry `roles: string[]`/`permsHash` but not a resolved permission list, so there's no cheap way today to ask "does this caller hold `approvals:instance:decide`" from inside a controller method; revisit once a resolved-permission-set-on-request mechanism exists. (5) Out of scope, deliberately: SLA reminder/escalation dispatch (`appr_level.sla_hours`/`escalation` are stored, per FR-APPR-005.1, but nothing acts on them — no scheduler/worker exists in this codebase, same "config exists, dispatcher doesn't" pattern as `comm_trigger_binding`); the `ApprovalDecidedEvent` has no subscriber yet (no dispatcher exists, same as every other module's outbox events so far); delegation chain-following beyond one hop (see `DelegationsService`'s doc comment for the rationale).

**Numbering allocator design note for future readers**: `NumberingService.allocate(em, docType, seriesCode)` takes the caller's `EntityManager`, never opens its own transaction, and uses `Repository.findOne({ where, lock: { mode: "pessimistic_write" } })` (real `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`) on `(doc_type, series_code, period_key)`. First-use auto-creates a `NEVER`-policy row via `save()` inside a try/catch that falls through to the same locked read on a Postgres unique-violation (23505) — i.e. upsert-on-first-use without ever needing an app-level check-then-write. The integration test explicitly runs its 25 concurrent transactions at READ COMMITTED rather than `tx()`'s REPEATABLE READ default — under REPEATABLE READ, Postgres raises `40001 could not serialize access due to concurrent update` when a blocked `FOR UPDATE` unblocks onto a row a concurrent transaction already committed (see PG docs §13.2.2), so **every real caller of `allocate()` that itself transacts at REPEATABLE READ (this codebase's `tx()` default) needs its own retry-on-40001 policy** — same as any other hot-row write under that isolation level. This is a caller-level concern, deliberately not solved inside `NumberingService` itself; flag it when building the first module (billing) that calls `allocate()` in anger.

**Accounting Core module design notes for future readers**: (1) `PostingService.post()`'s public API surface — `post(em: EntityManager, draft: PostJournalDraft): Promise<GlJournalWithLines>` and `reverse(em, journalId, narration, postedBy): Promise<GlJournalWithLines>`, both taking the caller's own `EntityManager` and opening no transaction of their own (same pattern as `NumberingService.allocate()`/`ApprovalEngineService.submit()`) — is load-bearing for every future domain module (billing, payments, wallet, procurement, inventory, expenses, payroll, banking, fixed-assets all call it inside their own business transaction to record a journal). (2) The writer-guard mechanism: `post()` reaches `em.queryRunner` directly and issues `SET LOCAL application_name = 'kfe-posting-service'` before any insert to `gl_journal`/`gl_journal_line`/`gl_period_account_total` — `SET LOCAL` is transaction-scoped, safe with pooled connections, and resets automatically at COMMIT/ROLLBACK; calling `post()`/`reverse()` with an `EntityManager` that has no attached `QueryRunner` (i.e. not inside an open transaction) throws immediately. (3) SOFT_CLOSED policy (ordinary MANUAL/SYSTEM/REVERSING blocked, CLOSING/OPENING allowed) is this pass's own judgement call — the DDL/triggers only encode HARD_CLOSED enforcement. (4) `gl_period_account_total` increment math uses a locked read (`findOneForUpdate`, a new repository method) then insert-on-first-use with a unique-violation retry, mirroring `NumberingService.allocate()`'s own hot-row-locking pattern exactly — multiple lines in one journal touching the same `(account, cost_center)` are pre-aggregated in memory before a single DB round-trip per key. (5) Out of scope, deliberately: opening-balance seed data (would need the writer-guard workaround plus real balances that don't exist yet); automatic `BudgetsService.onApprovalDecided()` wiring off `ApprovalEngineService.decide()` (no event dispatcher exists anywhere in this codebase yet); a `GL_BUDGET` `appr_workflow_def` seed row (deferred to whichever future pass first needs `submitForApproval()` to actually succeed).

## packages/contracts — 2026-07-28

**Verdict: `packages/contracts` now exists and delivers both halves in scope — OpenAPI-derived TS types + a typed client (Part 1), and a full zod schema mirroring every class-validator DTO across all 21 backend modules (Part 2), both real and verified, neither a worked example.** `apps/api` was booted specifically to dump its live OpenAPI document (509 routes, confirmed against the "apps/api Composition Root" section above), saved as `packages/contracts/openapi.json` (451,237 bytes, committed), and the api process was fully shut down afterward — this package's own `generate` script never needs a live server again.

### Part 1 — OpenAPI types + typed client
`openapi-typescript@7.13.0` (devDependency) generates `packages/contracts/src/generated/openapi-types.ts` (~27.7k lines: `paths`/`components`/`operations` interfaces for all 509 routes) from `openapi.json`. `openapi-fetch@0.17.0` (dependency) — openapi-typescript's own companion library, a ~1kb zero-dependency runtime that turns the generated `paths` type into full request/response type inference — is wrapped by a thin `src/client.ts` (`createApiClient(options)`), deliberately not a hand-rolled fetch wrapper per the task brief's own instruction. Neither package conflicts with anything already pinned elsewhere in the monorepo (nothing else in the workspace depended on either).

**Real upstream data-quality defect found and worked around, not silently patched**: the raw OpenAPI document is not spec-valid as-is — `@nestjs/swagger` auto-derives every `operationId` as `${ControllerClassName}_${methodName}` with no module qualification, and several controller class names repeat verbatim across unrelated modules (`AccountsController` in both `accounting` and `banking`; `CategoriesController` in `inventory`/`expenses`/`fixed-assets`; `TransfersController` in `banking`/`fixed-assets`) — this is the other half of the "Duplicate DTO detected" warnings `apps/api`'s own boot log already showed. OpenAPI 3.x requires `operationId` to be unique document-wide; this document has 16 collisions, so `openapi-typescript` faithfully emitted the same property key twice (real, different shapes) inside its one generated `operations` interface, which `tsc --noEmit` correctly rejected (TS2300/TS2717). Fixing this upstream would mean renaming controller classes across several already-complete, already-tested `packages/server` modules — explicitly out of this task's scope ("you should not need to modify anything under packages/server"). Fixed instead at the generation boundary only: `packages/contracts/scripts/generate-types.ts` reads `openapi.json` (which stays exactly as fetched, untouched, a faithful record), builds an in-memory deduplicated copy (colliding operationIds get their own module's path segment appended — `AccountsController_list` → `AccountsController_list__banking` for the second occurrence — deterministic, and can never itself collide since it's derived from the operation's own unique path), and feeds only that copy to `openapi-typescript`'s programmatic API (`openapiTS()`/`astToString()`, not the CLI, specifically so this transform could be inserted without a second on-disk file). `generate:types` logs every rename it makes.

`pnpm --filter contracts run generate` runs `generate:types` then `generate:zod` (Part 2, below) — both steps operate purely on already-saved files (`openapi.json` on disk, `packages/server/src/**/*.dto.ts` source), no live server required, exactly as scoped. Regenerating `openapi.json` itself (i.e. picking up NEW routes) is documented as the one manual/CI step this script deliberately does not do itself: boot `apps/api`, `curl http://localhost:3000/api/docs-json > packages/contracts/openapi.json`, shut it down, then run `generate`.

### Part 2 — full zod DTO coverage, via reflection-based codegen
**Scale found by real investigation, not estimated**: `Glob packages/server/src/**/dto/*.dto.ts` found 193 files; a broader `grep -r "class.*Dto"` outside `dto/` folders (per this task's own instruction to check) found exactly one more — `packages/server/src/shared/pagination/pagination.dto.ts` (`PaginationQueryDto`, used by every list endpoint's query params) — for **194 DTO files total**, containing **433 exported `*Dto` classes** (`grep -c "^export class \w*Dto"`), validating **2147 properties**. `licensing` (the one structurally-isolated module) has zero DTO classes — its mutual-auth API is JWS/interface-typed, not class-validator-DTO-typed, confirmed by grep before concluding "no coverage needed there."

**Why reflection/codegen over hand-authoring** (this task's own strong preference, confirmed the right call once the real scale was known): 433 classes by hand would mean either an unsustainable amount of manual retyping (violating this codebase's own "single source of truth, never hand-retype a shape that already exists elsewhere" convention — the same principle behind `AppDataSource.options.entities` being imported rather than restated, cited in the apps/api Composition Root section above) or an under-covered "worked example." Built instead: a small mechanical translator (`packages/contracts/codegen/`) that (1) source-scans every DTO file with the TypeScript Compiler API (`dto-scan.ts` — gives the ground-truth class/property list, `@Type(() => X)` nested-class references, `extends` base classes, and import statements, since several DTO class names — `CreateCategoryDto`, `CategoryResponseDto`, `UpdateCategoryDto`, `SpendDto`, `RunDueDto` — repeat verbatim across unrelated modules, the exact set `apps/api`'s own Swagger boot warnings already flagged, so class names alone are not a safe lookup key); (2) for each class, `require()`s the real DTO file and reads its ACTUAL runtime class-validator metadata via `getMetadataStorage().getTargetValidationMetadatas(ctor, "", false, false)` — the reflection API this task's brief named — which is the authoritative source for already-*evaluated* constraint values (`@MaxLength(120)`'s `120`, `@IsEnum(X)`'s resolved legal-value list, `@Matches(/.../)`'s compiled `RegExp`, `@IsIn([...])`'s array) rather than re-parsing decorator argument source text by hand; (3) mechanically translates each property's metadata into an equivalent zod validator chain (`codegen/decorator-to-zod.ts`), falling back to a TS-type-derived zod expression only for properties with zero class-validator decorators (common on `*-response.dto.ts` read-model classes, which validate nothing and exist purely to shape Swagger output) — this fallback path also resolves cross-file type aliases/`as const` arrays back to their real values (`codegen/external-type-resolver.ts`, e.g. `PyrlComponentKind`/`(typeof DECISIONS)[number]`) rather than degrading straight to `z.unknown()`; (4) emits one `*.schema.ts` file per source `*.dto.ts` file, mirroring `packages/server/src/{platform,domains,accounting}/*/api/dto/x.dto.ts` → `packages/contracts/src/{platform,domains,accounting}/*/x.schema.ts` (the `api/dto/` segment dropped as redundant, documented and codified once in `codegen/naming.ts`, imported by both the generator and the completeness test so the convention can never drift between them).

**Real edge cases the mechanical translator handles, not skirted around**: `AccountTreeNodeResponseDto extends AccountResponseDto { children: AccountTreeNodeResponseDto[] }` is a genuinely self-referential response DTO — the generator detects this (any property resolving back to the class currently being emitted) and switches that one class's emission from the normal `export const XSchema = ...; export type X = z.infer<typeof XSchema>;` pattern to an explicit TS `interface` (built from the DTO's own already-valid property type text) plus a `z.ZodType<X>` type annotation on the const, wrapping the self-reference in `z.lazy()` — the documented zod pattern for recursive schemas, needed because TypeScript cannot otherwise infer a schema's own type from an initializer that refers to itself. `extends` in general (3 classes: `AccountTreeNodeResponseDto`, `ReceiptDetailResponseDto`, `InstanceDetailResponseDto`) compiles to zod's `.merge()`. `@IsEnum(PAY_RECEIPT_SPLIT_METHODS)`/`@IsEnum(REVERSAL_REASON_CODES)` pass a plain `as const` array rather than a real TS `enum` — class-validator's own `validEnumValues()` helper (used to populate `constraints[1]`) only understands real enum objects and silently returns `[]` for an array argument, a genuine class-validator quirk, not a codegen bug; the translator falls back to `constraints[0]` (the array itself) in that case. Duplicate class names across unrelated modules are disambiguated by the referencing file's own same-file-then-import resolution (`decorator-to-zod.ts`'s `resolveClassRef`) rather than a flat name lookup, and the generated barrel (`src/index.ts`) applies the same discipline: files whose exports are still unclaimed get a flat `export *`; the first later file to collide with an already-claimed name is re-exported under a path-derived namespace instead (e.g. `export * as domains_inventory_category_schema from "./domains/inventory/category.schema"`) — no schema is ever silently dropped from the barrel, and every schema remains reachable by its unambiguous deep path (`@klickit/contracts/domains/inventory/category.schema`, the same `tsconfig.base.json` `paths` alias convention `@klickit/server/*` already established) regardless.

**Completeness verification — a real mechanism, not a manual claim**: `packages/contracts/src/__tests__/completeness.spec.ts` independently RE-SCANS every DTO file (reusing `dto-scan.ts`, but with no memory of what the generator wrote), then for each of the 433 classes: asserts its expected generated schema file exists and loads; asserts it exports a real zod schema under the expected name (`codegen/naming.ts`'s convention, not re-derived independently); asserts the schema's `.shape` keys (unwrapped through `.merge()`/`z.lazy()` where needed) are EXACTLY the DTO's own property names — INCLUDING inherited ones for the 3 `extends` cases, computed via the test's own independent re-implementation of the same same-file/import disambiguation rule the generator uses, so a shared bug can't cancel itself out between the two. **Result: 867/867 assertions passing** (433 classes × 2 checks + 1 sanity-count guard). The generator's own run log is separately real evidence too: **433 DTO classes found, 433 zod schemas generated, 2147 properties translated, 0 falling back to an untranslated decorator** (every `IsString`/`IsUUID`/`IsEmail`/`IsEnum`/`IsIn`/`IsNumber`/`IsInt`/`IsBoolean`/`IsArray`/`ArrayNotEmpty`/`ArrayMinSize`/`ArrayMaxSize`/`ValidateNested`/`MaxLength`/`MinLength`/`Length`/`Matches`/`Min`/`Max`/`IsOptional`/`IsDateString`/`IsISO8601`/`IsPositive`/`IsNegative`/`IsNotEmpty`/`IsIp`/`IsObject`/`IsNotEmptyObject`/`IsBooleanString`/`IsNumberString`/`IsLatitude`/`IsLongitude`/`IsJSON`/`IsMobilePhone`/`IsPhoneNumber`/`IsMongoId`/`IsHexColor`/`IsTimeZone`/`IsMimeType` decorator actually used anywhere in the 194 DTO files translated to a real, precise zod validator — earlier iterations of the translator fell back for 37 properties [`isIso8601`/`isIp` unmapped, the `IsEnum`-with-plain-array quirk above, and several `*-response.dto.ts` type-alias/const-array references]; each was closed by a real fix, not by relaxing the check).

### Files built
- `packages/contracts/package.json`/`tsconfig.json`/`tsconfig.codegen.json`/`jest.config.js` — new package, `@klickit/contracts`, workspace-registered.
- `packages/contracts/openapi.json` — committed, the exact document `apps/api`'s `/api/docs-json` returned (509 paths), untouched by the operationId-dedup workaround.
- `packages/contracts/scripts/generate-types.ts` — Part 1, see above.
- `packages/contracts/src/generated/openapi-types.ts` — generated (do-not-edit banner), ~27.7k lines.
- `packages/contracts/src/client.ts` — the `openapi-fetch` wrapper (Part 1).
- `packages/contracts/codegen/dto-scan.ts`, `naming.ts`, `decorator-to-zod.ts`, `external-type-resolver.ts`, `generate-zod-schemas.ts` — the Part 2 codegen pipeline.
- `packages/contracts/src/{platform,domains,accounting,shared}/**/*.schema.ts` — 194 generated files (do-not-edit banner), one per source `*.dto.ts` file, 433 zod schemas total.
- `packages/contracts/src/index.ts` — generated barrel (Part 1 exports + collision-aware Part 2 re-exports).
- `packages/contracts/src/__tests__/completeness.spec.ts` — the real completeness verification mechanism (867 assertions, all passing).
- `package.json` scripts: `generate:types`, `generate:zod`, `generate` (chains both), `build`, `typecheck`, `test`, `lint`.
